## ASSISTANT
Here’s an analysis of whether anything is missing and if locks are required in your `CustomerRepository`:

---

### **1. Is Anything Missing?**
#### **Validation for Data Integrity**
- **Issue**: Methods like `UpdateCustomerAsync` and `UpdateCustomerContactsAsync` assume the data being passed is valid.
- **Recommendation**: Add validation logic to ensure:
  - Data conforms to expected formats (e.g., valid `JSON` for `CustomerBillingContactsJson`).
  - Required fields in `CustomerEntity` are not null or empty.
  - Example:
    ```csharp
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(customer.AccountName)) 
        throw new ArgumentException("Account name cannot be null or empty.", nameof(customer.AccountName));
    ```

---

### **2. Locks: Are They Necessary?**
Locks are typically required in multi-threaded environments or when multiple processes might access or modify shared resources simultaneously.

#### **When Locks Are Not Necessary**
1. **Database Handles Concurrency**:
   - EF Core and the underlying database already handle concurrency at the transaction level.
   - Example: Updates are atomic, and EF Core ensures integrity with optimistic concurrency.

2. **Single-Threaded Application**:
   - If your application only processes one operation at a time for a given customer, no explicit locks are needed.

#### **When Locks Are Necessary**
Locks are useful if:
1. **High Contention on Resources**:
   - Multiple threads or processes might simultaneously update the same `CustomerEntity` or `CustomerBillingContactsJson`.
   - Example: Two updates to the same customer might overwrite each other.

2. **Manual Concurrency Handling**:
   - If optimistic concurrency is not sufficient, you can use locks to ensure mutual exclusion.

---

### **Implementation of Locks**
If you anticipate concurrent access, you can use a `SemaphoreSlim` to synchronize access at the repository level:

#### **Example: Adding a SemaphoreSlim**
```csharp
private static readonly SemaphoreSlim _customerLock = new SemaphoreSlim(1, 1);

public async Task<bool> UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(Guid customerId, string billingContactsJson)
{
    if (customerId == Guid.Empty) throw new ArgumentException("Customer ID cannot be empty.", nameof(customerId));
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(billingContactsJson)) throw new ArgumentException("Billing contacts JSON cannot be null or empty.", nameof(billingContactsJson));

    await _customerLock.WaitAsync(); // Acquire the lock
    try
    {
        var customer = await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customerId);
        if (customer == null) return false;

        customer.CustomerBillingContactsJson = billingContactsJson;
        await _context.SaveChangesAsync();
        return true;
    }
    finally
    {
        _customerLock.Release(); // Release the lock
    }
}
```

---

### **3. Other Considerations**
#### **Optimistic Concurrency**
EF Core supports optimistic concurrency control using a `RowVersion` column:
- Add a `byte[] RowVersion` property to `CustomerEntity`.
- Configure it in the `DbContext`:
  ```csharp
  modelBuilder.Entity<CustomerEntity>()
      .Property(c => c.RowVersion)
      .IsRowVersion();
  ```
- EF will throw a `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` if two operations conflict.

#### **Error Handling**
- **Issue**: Some exceptions (e.g., `DbUpdateException`) are not caught.
- **Recommendation**: Wrap database operations in `try-catch` blocks to handle exceptions gracefully.
- Example:
  ```csharp
  try
  {
      await _context.SaveChangesAsync();
  }
  catch (DbUpdateException ex)
  {
      // Log and handle the exception
      return false;
  }
  ```

---

### **Recommendations Summary**
1. **Validation**:
   - Add checks for required fields in `CustomerEntity` and valid JSON for `CustomerBillingContactsJson`.

2. **Locks**:
   - Use `SemaphoreSlim` if concurrent operations on the same resource are likely.
   - For most scenarios, optimistic concurrency with EF Core is sufficient.

3. **Error Handling**:
   - Wrap `SaveChangesAsync` calls in `try-catch` blocks to manage exceptions like `DbUpdateException`.

## ASSISTANT
The API handles concurrent incoming requests by design, as **ASP.NET Core** and **Entity Framework Core** are thread-safe at the request level. However, you may need to consider scenarios where multiple requests modify the same data, leading to **race conditions** or **conflicting updates**.

Here's how to modify your repository to better handle **multi-user scenarios** while ensuring data integrity:

---

### **Concurrency Handling Improvements**
1. **Optimistic Concurrency**:
   - Use EF Core's **RowVersion** feature to detect conflicting updates.

2. **Thread-Safe Access to Critical Sections**:
   - Employ **locks** or **semaphores** if multiple threads could modify the same resource in a shared memory context.

---

### **Updated Code: Implementing Optimistic Concurrency**

#### **Step 1: Update `CustomerEntity` for RowVersion**
Add a `RowVersion` property to detect conflicts:
```csharp
public class CustomerEntity
{
    public Guid Id { get; set; }
    public string AccountNumber { get; set; }
    public string AccountName { get; set; } = string.Empty;
    public string CustomerType { get; set; }
    public string AccountFullName { get; set; }
    public string AccountText { get; set; }
    public DateTime DateCreated { get; set; }
    public string CustomerBillingContactsJson { get; set; }

    [Timestamp] // EF Core will use this for concurrency checks
    public byte[] RowVersion { get; set; }
}
```

#### **Step 2: Modify `UpdateCustomerAsync` for Conflict Detection**
Throw a `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` if another user modifies the data:
```csharp
public async Task<bool> UpdateCustomerAsync(CustomerEntity customer)
{
    if (customer == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customer));

    var existingCustomer = await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customer.Id);
    if (existingCustomer == null) return false;

    // Set current values and keep RowVersion for concurrency checks
    _context.Entry(existingCustomer).CurrentValues.SetValues(customer);

    try
    {
        await _context.SaveChangesAsync();
        return true;
    }
    catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)
    {
        // Handle conflict, e.g., log it or notify the user
        throw new InvalidOperationException("The customer was updated by another user.");
    }
}
```

#### **Step 3: Modify `UpdateCustomerContactsAsync` for RowVersion**
Use the same mechanism to handle JSON updates:
```csharp
public async Task<bool> UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(Guid customerId, string billingContactsJson)
{
    if (customerId == Guid.Empty) throw new ArgumentException("Customer ID cannot be empty.", nameof(customerId));
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(billingContactsJson)) throw new ArgumentException("Billing contacts JSON cannot be null or empty.", nameof(billingContactsJson));

    var customer = await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customerId);
    if (customer == null) return false;

    customer.CustomerBillingContactsJson = billingContactsJson;

    try
    {
        await _context.SaveChangesAsync();
        return true;
    }
    catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)
    {
        throw new InvalidOperationException("The customer contacts were updated by another user.");
    }
}
```

---

### **When to Use Locks**
If operations span multiple repository methods or involve shared memory (e.g., caching):
- Add `SemaphoreSlim` locks for thread synchronization.
- Example:
    ```csharp
    private static readonly SemaphoreSlim _lock = new SemaphoreSlim(1, 1);

    public async Task<bool> UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(Guid customerId, string billingContactsJson)
    {
        await _lock.WaitAsync();
        try
        {
            return await UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(customerId, billingContactsJson);
        }
        finally
        {
            _lock.Release();
        }
    }
    ```

---

### **Other Considerations**
1. **Database Transaction Scope**:
   - Use EF Core's transactions to ensure atomicity for complex operations:
     ```csharp
     using var transaction = await _context.Database.BeginTransactionAsync();
     try
     {
         // Perform multiple operations
         await _context.SaveChangesAsync();
         await transaction.CommitAsync();
     }
     catch
     {
         await transaction.RollbackAsync();
         throw;
     }
     ```

2. **Conflict Notifications**:
   - Consider notifying users of conflicts and providing options (e.g., reload data, retry).

---

### **Key Takeaways**
1. **API-Level Stacking**:
   - The API and EF Core manage concurrent incoming requests effectively without locks for standard scenarios.
2. **Optimistic Concurrency**:
   - Recommended for handling conflicting updates with minimal overhead.
3. **Locks**:
   - Use only when thread-level synchronization is required for shared resources.

   ```csharp
       public string AccountName { get; set; } = string.Empty;
       public string CustomerType { get; set; }
       public string AccountFullName { get; set; }
       public string AccountText { get; set; }
       public DateTime DateCreated { get; set; }
       public string CustomerBillingContactsJson { get; set; }

       [Timestamp]
       public byte[] RowVersion { get; set; }
   }
   ```

2. **Ensure EF Core Tracks `RowVersion` Automatically**:
   - During updates, EF Core checks the current `RowVersion` in the database against the one provided by the user.
   - A `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` is thrown if there’s a mismatch.

---

### **2. Integrate `RowVersion` Explicitly in the Repository**
If you want more explicit control:
- Compare `RowVersion` manually before updates and handle conflicts.
- Example for `UpdateCustomerAsync`:
   ```csharp
   public async Task<bool> UpdateCustomerAsync(CustomerEntity customer)
   {
       if (customer == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customer));

       var existingCustomer = await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>()
           .FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customer.Id);

       if (existingCustomer == null) return false;

       // Ensure the RowVersion matches
       if (!existingCustomer.RowVersion.SequenceEqual(customer.RowVersion))
           throw new InvalidOperationException("The customer was updated by another user.");

       _context.Entry(existingCustomer).CurrentValues.SetValues(customer);

       try
       {
           await _context.SaveChangesAsync();
           return true;
       }
       catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)
       {
           throw new InvalidOperationException("A concurrency conflict occurred while updating the customer.");
       }
   }
   ```

---

### **3. Drop `RowVersion` if Not Needed**
If you decide **RowVersion** adds unnecessary complexity:
1. Remove `[Timestamp]` from the entity.
2. Let EF Core handle optimistic concurrency solely based on the `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` mechanism.

---

### **SignalR Integration**
Regardless of whether you use `RowVersion`:
- If a conflict occurs (e.g., a `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` is caught), you can use SignalR to notify the client:
   ```csharp
   catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)
   {
       await _signalRHub.Clients.User(callerId).SendAsync("ConcurrencyConflict", "Another user updated this record. Please refresh.");
       throw;
   }
   ```

---

### **Recommendation**
- **Keep `RowVersion`**: If your application has high concurrency or needs fine-grained conflict resolution, let EF Core handle concurrency via the `RowVersion`.
- **Drop `RowVersion`**: If conflicts are rare and EF Core's default mechanisms are sufficient.

## ASSISTANT
If you leave the `[Timestamp]` `RowVersion` property in the `CustomerEntity`, **Entity Framework Core will handle concurrency checks automatically**, as long as the `DbContext.SaveChangesAsync` method is used. No further updates to the repository are necessary.

Here's how EF Core handles it:
1. When an update occurs, EF Core includes the `RowVersion` value in the `WHERE` clause of the `UPDATE` SQL statement.
2. If another update modifies the same record (and its `RowVersion` changes) before the current update is committed, the `WHERE` clause fails to match, and EF Core throws a `DbUpdateConcurrencyException`.
3. You catch and handle this exception in your repository methods, as already implemented.

### **What EF Core Does Automatically**
- Tracks the `RowVersion` column during updates.
- Ensures only one successful update per `RowVersion`.
- Rolls back uncommitted changes if a conflict occurs.

### **Do You Need to Update the Repository?**
No, your current repository implementation already catches `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` in methods like `UpdateCustomerAsync` and `UpdateCustomerContactsAsync`. This aligns perfectly with the behavior of the `RowVersion` property.

### **What to Do Next**
- Keep the `[Timestamp]` `RowVersion` property in the `CustomerEntity`.
- Ensure the database schema includes a `RowVersion` column with a binary data type.
- No additional repository updates are required unless you want to customize the handling of `DbUpdateConcurrencyException` further, such as logging or notifying clients via SignalR.

To add **endpoints** for the repository methods, we can create an API controller that maps HTTP requests to these methods. Here's how to structure a `CustomersController` in ASP.NET Core to expose these functionalities.

---

### **CustomersController Implementation**

```csharp
using Logistics.Application.Models.Customers;
using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Customers;
using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Mvc;
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Threading.Tasks;

namespace Logistix.ServerAPI.Controllers
{
    [ApiController]
    [Route("api/[controller]")]
    public class CustomersController : ControllerBase
    {
        private readonly CustomerRepository _customerRepository;

        public CustomersController(CustomerRepository customerRepository)
        {
            _customerRepository = customerRepository ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customerRepository));
        }

        // POST: api/customers
        [HttpPost]
        public async Task<IActionResult> AddCustomer([FromBody] CustomerEntity customer)
        {
            if (customer == null) return BadRequest("Customer cannot be null.");
            
            var result = await _customerRepository.AddCustomerAsync(customer);
            return result ? Ok("Customer added successfully.") : StatusCode(500, "An error occurred while adding the customer.");
        }

        // GET: api/customers/{id}
        [HttpGet("{id}")]
        public async Task<IActionResult> GetCustomerById(Guid id)
        {
            if (id == Guid.Empty) return BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");

            var customer = await _customerRepository.GetCustomerByIdAsync(id);
            return customer != null ? Ok(customer) : NotFound("Customer not found.");
        }

        // GET: api/customers
        [HttpGet]
        public async Task<IActionResult> GetCustomersPaged([FromQuery] int pageNumber = 1, [FromQuery] int pageSize = 10)
        {
            if (pageNumber <= 0 || pageSize <= 0) return BadRequest("Page number and size must be greater than zero.");

            var customers = await _customerRepository.GetCustomersPagedAsync(pageNumber, pageSize);
            return Ok(customers);
        }

        // PUT: api/customers
        [HttpPut]
        public async Task<IActionResult> UpdateCustomer([FromBody] CustomerEntity customer)
        {
            if (customer == null) return BadRequest("Customer cannot be null.");

            try
            {
                var result = await _customerRepository.UpdateCustomerAsync(customer);
                return result ? Ok("Customer updated successfully.") : NotFound("Customer not found.");
            }
            catch (InvalidOperationException ex)
            {
                return Conflict(ex.Message);
            }
        }

        // PUT: api/customers/{id}/contacts
        [HttpPut("{id}/contacts")]
        public async Task<IActionResult> UpdateCustomerContacts(Guid id, [FromBody] string billingContactsJson)
        {
            if (id == Guid.Empty) return BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");
            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(billingContactsJson)) return BadRequest("Billing contacts JSON cannot be empty.");

            try
            {
                var result = await _customerRepository.UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(id, billingContactsJson);
                return result ? Ok("Customer contacts updated successfully.") : NotFound("Customer not found.");
            }
            catch (InvalidOperationException ex)
            {
                return Conflict(ex.Message);
            }
        }

        // DELETE: api/customers/{id}
        [HttpDelete("{id}")]
        public async Task<IActionResult> DeleteCustomer(Guid id)
        {
            if (id == Guid.Empty) return BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");

            try
            {
                var result = await _customerRepository.DeleteCustomerAsync(id);
                return result ? Ok("Customer deleted successfully.") : NotFound("Customer not found.");
            }
            catch (InvalidOperationException ex)
            {
                return Conflict(ex.Message);
            }
        }

        // GET: api/customers/search/accountnumber
        [HttpGet("search/accountnumber")]
        public async Task<IActionResult> GetCustomerByAccountNumber([FromQuery] string accountNumber)
        {
            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) return BadRequest("Account number cannot be null or empty.");

            var customer = await _customerRepository.GetCustomerByAccountNumberAsync(accountNumber);
            return customer != null ? Ok(customer) : NotFound("Customer not found.");
        }

        // GET: api/customers/search/accountname
        [HttpGet("search/accountname")]
        public async Task<IActionResult> SearchCustomersByAccountName([FromQuery] string accountName)
        {
            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountName)) return BadRequest("Account name cannot be null or empty.");

            var customers = await _customerRepository.SearchCustomersByAccountNameAsync(accountName);
            return Ok(customers);
        }
    }
}
```

---

### **Key Features of the Controller**
1. **CRUD Endpoints**:
   - Add, retrieve, update, and delete customers.
   - Separate endpoints for updating contacts.

2. **Pagination Support**:
   - Retrieves a paginated list of customers via `GetCustomersPaged`.

3. **Search Functionality**:
   - Search by account number or partial account name.

4. **Concurrency Conflict Handling**:
   - Returns a `409 Conflict` status when concurrency issues arise.

---

### **Next Steps**
1. **Wire Up the Controller**:
   - Ensure dependency injection for `CustomerRepository` is configured in `Startup.cs`:
     ```csharp
     services.AddScoped<CustomerRepository>();
     ```

2. **Test Endpoints**:
   - Use tools like **Postman** or **Swagger** to test the API.

3. **SignalR Integration**:
   - If needed, extend the methods to notify clients via SignalR on data updates or conflicts.

Yes, the updated minimal API **uses dependency injection (DI)** for the `CustomerRepository`. The repository is registered as a service, and it is injected into the endpoints as needed.

Here’s how DI works in this case:
1. **Registering the Repository**:
   Ensure the `CustomerRepository` is registered in `builder.Services`:
   ```csharp
   builder.Services.AddTransient<CustomerRepository>();
   ```

2. **Using the Repository in Endpoints**:

   ```csharp
   async (CustomerEntity customer, CustomerRepository customerRepository)
   ```

This pattern ensures that the repository’s lifetime is managed by the framework, and you don’t need to manually create or dispose of its instances.

builder.Services.AddHttpsRedirection(options =>
{
    options.RedirectStatusCode = StatusCodes.Status307TemporaryRedirect;
    options.HttpsPort = 443;
});

builder.Services.AddSignalR(options =>
{
    // Reduce the time interval between server pings
    options.KeepAliveInterval = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10); // Ping every 10 seconds

    // Reduce the client timeout interval
    options.ClientTimeoutInterval = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(20); // Mark disconnected if no response within 20 seconds
});

// Register repositories and services for dependency injection
builder.Services.AddTransient<ILoggingRepository, LoggingRepository>();
builder.Services.AddTransient<IPackageRepository, PackageRepository>();
builder.Services.AddTransient<IConnectionRepository, ConnectionRepository>();
builder.Services.AddTransient<IOnboardingRepository, OnboardingRepository>();
builder.Services.AddTransient<IAuthenticateService, AuthenticateService>();

// Register the background service to handle long-running tasks
builder.Services.AddHostedService<PostOfficeService>();

// Configure JWT authentication
builder.Services.AddAuthentication(o =>
{
    o.DefaultAuthenticateScheme = JwtBearerDefaults.AuthenticationScheme;
    o.DefaultChallengeScheme = JwtBearerDefaults.AuthenticationScheme;
}).AddJwtBearer(o =>
{
    o.Audience = "your-api-id";
    o.RequireHttpsMetadata = false;
});

var app = builder.Build();

app.UseSerilogRequestLogging();

// Truncate table when the application starts and log the action
using (var scope = app.Services.CreateScope())
{
    var repository = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<IConnectionRepository>();
    try
    {
        await repository.TruncateTable();
        Log.Information("Successfully truncated the connection table.");
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        Log.Error(ex, "Failed to truncate the connection table.");
    }
}

// Use custom middleware for logging requests and responses
app.UseMiddleware<RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware>();

if (app.Environment.IsDevelopment())
{
    app.UseDeveloperExceptionPage();
    app.UseSwagger();
    app.UseSwaggerUI(c =>
    {
        c.SwaggerEndpoint("/swagger/v1/swagger.json", "Logistics Mobile API V1");
        c.RoutePrefix = string.Empty;
    });

    app.Urls.Add("https://192.168.2.134:7072");
    app.Urls.Add("http://192.168.2.134:5072");
}
else
{
    app.UseExceptionHandler("/Error");
}

// Configure the request pipeline
app.MapRazorPages();
app.MapHub<SignalRDataRouter>("/data");
app.MapHub<SignalRChatRouter>("/audio");
app.UseRouting();
app.UseStaticFiles();
app.UseAuthentication();
app.UseAuthorization();

// Endpoint for user authentication
app.MapPost("/authenticate/",
[AllowAnonymous]
async (IAuthenticateService authenticateService, string apiKey, string appGUID) =>
{
    if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(apiKey))
    {
        Log.Warning("Authentication failed: API Key is empty.");
        return Results.Text("API Key is empty.");
    }

    try
    {
        var result = await authenticateService.LoginAsync(apiKey, appGUID);
        if (result.Succeeded)
        {
            Log.Information("User authenticated successfully.");
            return Results.Ok(result);
        }
        else
        {
            Log.Warning("Authentication failed for API Key {ApiKey}.", apiKey);
            return Results.BadRequest(result);
        }
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        Log.Error(ex, "Error during authentication.");
        return Results.StatusCode(500);
    }
});

// Endpoint for managing customers
app.MapPost("/customers",
    async (CustomerEntity customer, CustomerRepository customerRepository) =>
    {
        if (customer == null) return Results.BadRequest("Customer cannot be null.");

        try
        {
            var result = await customerRepository.AddCustomerAsync(customer);
            return result ? Results.Ok("Customer added successfully.") : Results.StatusCode(500, "Error adding customer.");
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while adding a customer.");
            return Results.StatusCode(500, "Internal Server Error.");
        }
    });

app.MapGet("/customers/{id:guid}",
    async (Guid id, CustomerRepository customerRepository) =>
    {
        if (id == Guid.Empty) return Results.BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");

        try
        {
            var customer = await customerRepository.GetCustomerByIdAsync(id);
            return customer != null ? Results.Ok(customer) : Results.NotFound("Customer not found.");
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            Log.Error(ex, "Error retrieving customer by ID.");
            return Results.StatusCode(500, "Internal Server Error.");
        }
    });

app.MapPut("/customers",
    async (CustomerEntity customer, CustomerRepository customerRepository) =>
    {
        if (customer == null) return Results.BadRequest("Customer cannot be null.");

        try
        {
            var result = await customerRepository.UpdateCustomerAsync(customer);
            return result ? Results.Ok("Customer updated successfully.") : Results.NotFound("Customer not found.");
        }
        catch (InvalidOperationException ex)
        {
            Log.Warning(ex, "Concurrency conflict occurred.");
            return Results.Conflict(ex.Message);
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            Log.Error(ex, "Error updating customer.");
            return Results.StatusCode(500, "Internal Server Error.");
        }
    });

app.MapPut("/customers/{id:guid}/contacts",
    async (Guid id, string billingContactsJson, CustomerRepository customerRepository) =>
    {
        if (id == Guid.Empty) return Results.BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");
        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(billingContactsJson)) return Results.BadRequest("Billing contacts JSON cannot be empty.");

        try
        {
            var result = await customerRepository.UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(id, billingContactsJson);
            return result ? Results.Ok("Customer contacts updated successfully.") : Results.NotFound("Customer not found.");
        }
        catch (InvalidOperationException ex)
        {
            Log.Warning(ex, "Concurrency conflict occurred while updating contacts.");
            return Results.Conflict(ex.Message);
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            Log.Error(ex, "Error updating customer contacts.");
            return Results.StatusCode(500, "Internal Server Error.");
        }
    });

app.MapDelete("/customers/{id:guid}",
    async (Guid id, CustomerRepository customerRepository) =>
    {
        if (id == Guid.Empty) return Results.BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");

        try
        {
            var result = await customerRepository.DeleteCustomerAsync(id);
            return result ? Results.Ok("Customer deleted successfully.") : Results.NotFound("Customer not found.");
        }
        catch (InvalidOperationException ex)
        {
            Log.Warning(ex, "Concurrency conflict occurred while deleting customer.");
            return Results.Conflict(ex.Message);
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            Log.Error(ex, "Error deleting customer.");
            return Results.StatusCode(500, "Internal Server Error.");
        }
    });

app.MapGet("/customers/search/accountnumber",
    async (string accountNumber, CustomerRepository customerRepository) =>
    {
        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) return Results.BadRequest("Account number cannot be null or empty.");

        try
        {
            var customer = await customerRepository.GetCustomerByAccountNumberAsync(accountNumber);
            return customer != null ? Results.Ok(customer) : Results.NotFound("Customer not found.");
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            Log.Error(ex, "Error searching customer by account number.");
            return Results.StatusCode(500, "Internal Server Error.");
        }
    });

app.MapGet("/customers/search/accountname",
    async (string accountName, CustomerRepository customerRepository) =>
    {
        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountName)) return Results.BadRequest("Account name cannot be null or empty.");

        try
        {
            var customers = await customerRepository.SearchCustomersByAccountNameAsync(accountName);
            return Results.Ok(customers);
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            Log.Error(ex, "Error searching customers by account name.");
            return Results.StatusCode(500, "Internal Server Error.");
        }
    });

app.Run();

These endpoints handle customer creation, retrieval, updates, deletion, and billing contact updates. Let me know if further refinements or additional endpoints are needed!

In Visual Studio, you can easily **reformat the code** for better readability after it has been compacted like this. Follow these steps:

3. **Enable Line Wrapping (Optional)**:
   - If the lines are still too long, enable **line wrapping** for easier reading:

     - Enable **"Word wrap"**.

4. **Check Code Style Settings** (Optional):

To build migrations for your `CustomerContext` in **Entity Framework Core**, follow these steps:

---

### **1. Add EF Core Tools**
Ensure that the **Entity Framework Core CLI tools** are installed in your project:
```bash
dotnet add package Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design
```

---

### **2. Configure `CustomerContext` in `Program.cs`**
Make sure your `CustomerContext` is registered in `Program.cs` for dependency injection:
```csharp
builder.Services.AddDbContext<CustomerContext>(options =>
    options.UseSqlServer(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("DefaultConnection")));
```

---

### **3. Create a Migration**
Run the following command in your **project directory** (where the `.csproj` file is located):
```bash
dotnet ef migrations add InitialCreate
```

This will:
1. Generate a folder named `Migrations` (if it doesn’t already exist).
2. Create a migration file that contains the SQL for creating your `CustomerQueue` table.

---

### **4. Apply the Migration**
Run the following command to apply the migration and create/update the database schema:
```bash
dotnet ef database update
```

---

### **5. Verify the Migration**
Check your database to ensure the `CustomerQueue` table is created with the appropriate columns.

---

### **Additional Commands**
- **List Migrations**:
  ```bash
  dotnet ef migrations list
  ```
- **Remove Last Migration** (if you want to undo):
  ```bash
  dotnet ef migrations remove
  ```

## USER
fix this with the new code i added using Logistix.ServerAPI.Data.Context;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Customers;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Models.Entity;

using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;

using System;

using System.Collections.Generic;

using System.Linq;

using System.Text.Json;

using System.Threading.Tasks;



namespace Logistix.ServerAPI.Data.Repository

{

    public class CustomerRepository : ICustomerRepository

    {

        private readonly IDbContextFactory<ConnectionsContext> _dbContextFactory;

        private readonly ILogger<ConnectionRepository> _logger;



       public CustomerRepository(IDbContextFactory<ConnectionsContext> dbContextFactory, ILogger<ConnectionRepository> logger)

        {

            _dbContextFactory = dbContextFactory;

            _logger = logger;

        }



        // Add a new customer

        public async Task<bool> AddCustomerAsync(CustomerEntity customer)

        {

            if (customer == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customer));



            _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().Add(customer);

            await _context.SaveChangesAsync();

            return true;

        }



        // Retrieve a customer by ID

        public async Task<CustomerEntity> GetCustomerByIdAsync(Guid customerId)

        {

            if (customerId == Guid.Empty) throw new ArgumentException("Customer ID cannot be empty.", nameof(customerId));



            return await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customerId);

        }



        // Retrieve all customers with optional pagination

        public async Task<IEnumerable<CustomerEntity>> GetCustomersPagedAsync(int pageNumber, int pageSize)

        {

            if (pageNumber <= 0 || pageSize <= 0) throw new ArgumentException("Page number and size must be greater than zero.");



            return await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>()

                .OrderBy(c => c.AccountName)

                .Skip((pageNumber - 1) * pageSize)

                .Take(pageSize)

                .ToListAsync();

        }



        // Update an existing customer

        public async Task<bool> UpdateCustomerAsync(CustomerEntity customer)

        {

            if (customer == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customer));



            var existingCustomer = await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customer.Id);

            if (existingCustomer == null) return false;



            _context.Entry(existingCustomer).CurrentValues.SetValues(customer);



            try

            {

                await _context.SaveChangesAsync();

                return true;

            }

            catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)

            {

                throw new InvalidOperationException("The customer was updated by another user.");

            }

        }



        // Update billing contacts JSON

        public async Task<bool> UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(Guid customerId, string billingContactsJson)

        {

            if (customerId == Guid.Empty) throw new ArgumentException("Customer ID cannot be empty.", nameof(customerId));

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(billingContactsJson)) throw new ArgumentException("Billing contacts JSON cannot be null or empty.", nameof(billingContactsJson));



            var customer = await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customerId);

            if (customer == null) return false;



            customer.CustomerBillingContactsJson = billingContactsJson;



            try

            {

                await _context.SaveChangesAsync();

                return true;

            }

            catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)

            {

                throw new InvalidOperationException("The customer contacts were updated by another user.");

            }

        }



        // Delete a customer by ID

        public async Task<bool> DeleteCustomerAsync(Guid customerId)

        {

            if (customerId == Guid.Empty) throw new ArgumentException("Customer ID cannot be empty.", nameof(customerId));



            var customer = await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.Id == customerId);

            if (customer == null) return false;



            _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().Remove(customer);



            try

            {

                await _context.SaveChangesAsync();

                return true;

            }

            catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)

            {

                throw new InvalidOperationException("The customer was deleted or updated by another user.");

            }

        }



        // Search customers by Account Number

        public async Task<CustomerEntity> GetCustomerByAccountNumberAsync(string accountNumber)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) throw new ArgumentException("Account number cannot be null or empty.", nameof(accountNumber));



            return await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.AccountNumber == accountNumber);

        }



        // Search customers by Account Name

        public async Task<IEnumerable<CustomerEntity>> SearchCustomersByAccountNameAsync(string accountName)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountName)) throw new ArgumentException("Account name cannot be null or empty.", nameof(accountName));



            return await _context.Set<CustomerEntity>()

                .Where(c => c.AccountName.Contains(accountName))

                .ToListAsync();

        }

    }

}

## ASSISTANT
To address the "trusted connection" issue in your connection strings, you can append the `TrustServerCertificate=True` parameter to each connection string. This explicitly tells the application to trust the SQL Server certificate, bypassing the trust issue.

### **Updated Connection Strings**
```json
{
    "ConnectionStrings": {
        "PostOfficeConnection": "Data Source=nexus-prime;Initial Catalog=logistx_postoffice;User Id=developer;Password=developer;TrustServerCertificate=True",
        "LoggingConnection": "Data Source=nexus-prime;Initial Catalog=logistx_logging;User Id=developer;Password=developer;TrustServerCertificate=True",
        "SignalRConnection": "Data Source=nexus-prime;Initial Catalog=logistx_signalr;User Id=developer;Password=developer;TrustServerCertificate=True",
        "OnboardingConnection": "Data Source=nexus-prime;Initial Catalog=logistx_onboarding;User Id=developer;Password=developer;TrustServerCertificate=True",
        "ServerConnection": "Data Source=nexus-prime;Initial Catalog=logistix_main;User Id=developer;Password=developer;TrustServerCertificate=True"
    }
}
```

### **What This Does**
- The `TrustServerCertificate=True` parameter bypasses the validation of the SQL Server’s SSL certificate.
- This is useful for development or environments where certificate validation isn't set up.

---

### **Steps to Implement**
1. Update your `appsettings.json` with the modified connection strings.
2. Ensure your `Program.cs` or `Startup.cs` uses these updated connection strings when configuring your DbContexts:
   ```csharp
   builder.Services.AddDbContext<PostOfficeContext>(options =>
       options.UseSqlServer(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("PostOfficeConnection")));
   ```

## USER
doing an access query i need to generate a guid and covnert to string to insert data, im moving it from access to this sql

## ASSISTANT
To move data from Access to SQL while generating a GUID and converting it to a string, you can use the following approach:

### **Steps**
1. Generate a GUID for each record.
2. Convert the GUID to a string format.
3. Insert the data into SQL Server.

Here’s an example query in Access VBA that handles this:

---

### **VBA Code for Access Query**
```vba
Sub MigrateDataToSQL()
    Dim db As DAO.Database
    Dim rs As DAO.Recordset
    Dim sql As String
    Dim guid As String

    ' Open Access database
    Set db = CurrentDb
    Set rs = db.OpenRecordset("SELECT * FROM AccessTable") ' Replace AccessTable with your Access table name

    ' Open a connection to SQL Server
    Dim conn As Object
    Set conn = CreateObject("ADODB.Connection")
    conn.Open "Driver={SQL Server};Server=nexus-prime;Database=logistix_main;Uid=developer;Pwd=developer;TrustServerCertificate=True;"

    ' Iterate through Access records and insert into SQL Server
    Do Until rs.EOF
        guid = Mid(CreateObject("Scriptlet.TypeLib").Guid, 2, 36) ' Generate GUID as string

        sql = "INSERT INTO Customers (Id, AccountNumber, AccountName, CustomerType, AccountFullName, AccountText, DateCreated) " & _
              "VALUES ('" & guid & "', '" & rs!AccountNumber & "', '" & rs!AccountName & "', '" & rs!CustomerType & "', '" & _
              rs!AccountFullName & "', '" & rs!AccountText & "', '" & Format(rs!DateCreated, "yyyy-mm-dd hh:nn:ss") & "')"

        conn.Execute sql
        rs.MoveNext
    Loop

    ' Close connections
    rs.Close
    conn.Close

    MsgBox "Data migrated successfully!"
End Sub
```

---

### **Key Points**
1. **GUID Generation**:
   - `Mid(CreateObject("Scriptlet.TypeLib").Guid, 2, 36)` generates a GUID and trims the curly braces.

2. **SQL Insert Query**:
   - The query inserts the GUID along with the other fields into the `Customers` table in SQL Server.

3. **SQL Server Connection**:
   - The `ADODB.Connection` object uses a connection string with `TrustServerCertificate=True` to bypass certificate validation issues.

4. **Date Conversion**:
   - Use `Format` to ensure the date is in the correct SQL format.

5. **Table Mapping**:
   - Ensure the field names in the SQL Server table (`Customers`) match those in the Access table.

---

Yes, in Microsoft Access, you can generate a GUID directly in a query using the built-in function `GenGUID()`. However, if you are designing the query to migrate data to SQL Server, here’s how to incorporate GUID generation:

---

### **Using Access Query Design**
1. **Create a Query**:
   - Open your Access database.
   - Go to the **Create** tab and select **Query Design**.

2. **Generate GUID in the Query**:
   - Use the `GenGUID()` function to generate GUIDs. For example:
     ```sql
     SELECT 
         GenGUID() AS ID, 
         [AccountNumber], 
         [AccountName], 
         [CustomerType], 
         [AccountFullName], 
         [AccountText], 
         [DateCreated] 
     FROM [AccessTable];
     ```
   - Replace `[AccessTable]` with your table name.

3. **Export to SQL Server**:
   - Use the **Export Wizard** in Access to move the query results to SQL Server.
   - Select **External Data > Export > ODBC Database**, choose your SQL Server connection, and export the query.

---

### **Alternative: Generate GUID in SQL Server**
If GUID generation can happen after data export to SQL Server, use the following SQL Server query to update the `Id` column with GUIDs:
```sql
UPDATE Customers
SET Id = NEWID()
WHERE Id IS NULL;
```

---

### **Key Considerations**
- **Avoid Duplicate GUIDs**:
   - Ensure each row in Access gets a unique GUID before exporting.
- **Consistency**:
   - Match the GUID field data type in SQL Server (`uniqueidentifier`) and Access (`String` if stored as text).

If you're using the `StringFromGUID` function in Access, here's how you can generate and use it directly in an **Access Query Design**:

---

### **Query Design Steps**
1. **Open Query Design**:
   - Go to **Create > Query Design** in Microsoft Access.
   - Add the relevant table(s).

2. **Generate GUID and Convert to String**:
   - In the **Field** row, add the following expression:
     ```sql
     StringFromGUID(GenGUID())
     ```
   - Example:
     ```sql
     SELECT StringFromGUID(GenGUID()) AS GeneratedGUID, AccountNumber, AccountName
     FROM YourTableName;
     ```

3. **Use the Query**:
   - You can now run this query to generate GUIDs as strings and include them in your results.

---

### **Export Data to SQL Server**
If you’re migrating to SQL Server:
1. Save the query with a descriptive name (e.g., `GenerateGUIDs`).
2. Use the **External Data** tab in Access:
   - Select **Export > ODBC Database**.
   - Choose the target SQL Server table and export.

---

### **Notes**
- `StringFromGUID` converts a GUID to a string representation.
- `GenGUID` generates a new GUID in Access.

## USER
does access have a feature to generate guid?  GenGUI() is not found

## ASSISTANT
Microsoft Access does not have a built-in query function to generate GUIDs directly. However, you can use **VBA functions** to generate GUIDs, which can then be incorporated into your Access queries.

### **Generate GUID in Access with VBA**
1. Open the **VBA Editor**:
   - Press `Alt + F11` in Access.
   
2. Create a VBA Function:
   ```vba
   Public Function GenerateGUID() As String
       GenerateGUID = Mid(CreateObject("Scriptlet.TypeLib").Guid, 2, 36)
   End Function
   ```

   - This generates a GUID as a 36-character string without the curly braces.

3. Use the Function in a Query:
   - Save the VBA function, then create an Access query using the function:
     ```sql
     SELECT GenerateGUID() AS NewGUID, * FROM YourTable;
     ```

4. Insert the Generated GUID into Your Query:
   - If you're moving data to SQL Server, include the GUID generation in your `INSERT` query:
     ```sql
     INSERT INTO YourSQLTable (Id, Column1, Column2)
     SELECT GenerateGUID(), Column1, Column2 FROM YourAccessTable;
     ```

---

### **Alternative: Generate GUID in SQL Server**
If you prefer to generate GUIDs directly in SQL Server after exporting data from Access:
- Use the `NEWID()` function in SQL Server:
  ```sql
  UPDATE YourSQLTable
  SET Id = NEWID()
  WHERE Id IS NULL;
  ```

---

### **Key Points**
- **Access**: Use a VBA function like `GenerateGUID()` if GUIDs are required during query execution.
- **SQL Server**: Use `NEWID()` to generate GUIDs after migrating data.

The `CreateObject("Scriptlet.TypeLib")` method can fail with "permission denied" due to system or security restrictions. If you cannot use this method, here’s how to proceed:

---

### **Option 1: Generate GUIDs Using VBA Without `Scriptlet.TypeLib`**
Use a simpler GUID generation method via `GUIDFromString`:
```vba
Public Function GenerateGUID() As String
    GenerateGUID = Mid(GUIDFromString(Application.NewGUID), 2, 36)
End Function
```

This method:
1. Uses `Application.NewGUID` to generate a GUID.
2. Trims unnecessary curly braces using `Mid`.

---

### **Option 2: Use SQL Server to Generate GUIDs**
If Access is only for exporting to SQL Server, generate GUIDs in SQL Server instead:
```sql
UPDATE Customers
SET Id = NEWID()
WHERE Id IS NULL;
```

---

### **Option 3: Use Access Query for Sequential GUIDs**
Access doesn’t natively support GUID generation in queries, but you can generate sequential numbers and convert them later into unique strings.

---

### **Why Use SQL Server’s `NEWID`?**
- Ensures GUIDs align with SQL Server's `uniqueidentifier`.
- Avoids compatibility issues when inserting into SQL Server.

## USER
how do i setup the database table then to generate guid's for autoincretment?


## ASSISTANT
To configure your SQL Server table to automatically generate GUIDs for the `Id` column, follow these steps:

---

### **1. Update the Table Schema**
Modify your SQL Server table to use the `NEWSEQUENTIALID()` or `NEWID()` function as the default value for the `Id` column.

```sql
ALTER TABLE CustomerQueue
ADD CONSTRAINT DF_CustomerQueue_Id DEFAULT NEWSEQUENTIALID() FOR Id;
```

- **`NEWSEQUENTIALID()`**: Generates sequential GUIDs, which are better for indexing and performance.
- **`NEWID()`**: Generates random GUIDs.

---

### **2. Update the Entity Configuration**
Update the `CustomerEntity` configuration in the `OnModelCreating` method to reflect the default value generation.

In the **`PostOfficeContext`**:
```csharp
modelBuilder.Entity<CustomerEntity>(entity =>
{
    entity.ToTable("CustomerQueue");

    entity.HasKey(e => e.Id).HasName("id");

    entity.Property(e => e.Id)
        .HasColumnName("id")
        .ValueGeneratedOnAdd()
        .HasDefaultValueSql("NEWSEQUENTIALID()");

    entity.Property(e => e.AccountNumber).HasColumnName("account_number").HasMaxLength(50);
    entity.Property(e => e.AccountName).HasColumnName("account_name").HasMaxLength(100);
    entity.Property(e => e.CustomerType).HasColumnName("customer_type").HasMaxLength(50);
    entity.Property(e => e.AccountFullName).HasColumnName("account_full_name").HasMaxLength(200);
    entity.Property(e => e.AccountText).HasColumnName("account_text").HasMaxLength(500);
    entity.Property(e => e.DateCreated).HasColumnName("date_created");
    entity.Property(e => e.CustomerBillingContactsJson).HasColumnName("billing_contacts_json").HasColumnType("nvarchar(max)");
    entity.Property(e => e.RowVersion).HasColumnName("row_version").IsRowVersion();
});
```

---

### **3. Migrate the Database**
Run the following commands to update your database schema:
```bash
dotnet ef migrations add AddDefaultGuid
dotnet ef database update
```

---

### **4. Optional: Generate GUID in Entity Framework**
If you want to generate GUIDs programmatically instead of relying on SQL Server defaults, you can update your `CustomerEntity` class:

```csharp
public class CustomerEntity
{
    public Guid Id { get; set; } = Guid.NewGuid(); // Generates GUID on object creation
    public string AccountNumber { get; set; }
    public string AccountName { get; set; } = string.Empty;
    public string CustomerType { get; set; }
    public string AccountFullName { get; set; }
    public string AccountText { get; set; }
    public DateTime DateCreated { get; set; }
    public string CustomerBillingContactsJson { get; set; }
}
```

This ensures GUIDs are generated on the application side instead of relying on the database.

---

### **Recommendation**
- Use **`NEWSEQUENTIALID()`** in SQL Server for performance in indexed tables.
- Combine it with EF Core’s `.HasDefaultValueSql("NEWSEQUENTIALID()")` for seamless integration.

## ASSISTANT
Yes, Microsoft Access has a **"Unique Values"** property (equivalent to `SELECT DISTINCT`) in its **Query Design View**. Here's how you can achieve the desired result in Access:

---

### **Steps to Use "Unique Values" in Access Query Design**
1. **Open Query Design**:
   - Go to **Create > Query Design** in Access.
   - Add the table (`dbo_ACT_FILE`) to the query.

2. **Enable Unique Values**:
   - Right-click anywhere in the query design area.
   - Select **Properties** (or press `Alt+Enter`).
   - Set the **"Unique Values"** property to `Yes`.

3. **Add Fields to the Query**:
   - Drag the fields you need (`ACTNUM`, `ACTNAME`, `CODE`, etc.) into the query grid.
   - Ensure the fields are arranged as needed for the `SELECT DISTINCT` logic.

4. **Run the Query**:
   - Click **Run** to execute the query.

This generates a query like:
```sql
SELECT DISTINCT ACTNUM, ACTNAME, CODE, ACCT_NAME, ACCT_TXT, ADD_DATE
FROM dbo_ACT_FILE
ORDER BY ACTNAME;
```

---

### **SQL Query in Access**
Alternatively, switch to **SQL View** and directly paste your query:
```sql
SELECT DISTINCT ACTNUM, ACTNAME, CODE, ACCT_NAME, ACCT_TXT, ADD_DATE
FROM dbo_ACT_FILE
ORDER BY ACTNAME;
```

---

### **Key Notes**
- **Unique Values vs. Unique Records**:
   - "Unique Values" (`DISTINCT`) ensures unique combinations of selected fields.
   - "Unique Records" (`DISTINCTROW`) ensures unique rows based on the entire record (including hidden fields).

## USER
i just want to filter out so only one account number will be selected, i have tons of multipe recrods that are the same other than adddate and i dont care i just need one per

## ASSISTANT
To filter your query so that only one row per `ACTNUM` (account number) is returned, you can achieve this by grouping the data and selecting the first (or max/min) record for each group. Here's how to do it:

---

### **Access Query Design Method**
1. **Open Query Design**:
   - Go to **Create > Query Design**.
   - Add your table (`dbo_ACT_FILE`).

2. **Set Up Grouping**:
   - Add the fields you want in your result (e.g., `ACTNUM`, `ACTNAME`, etc.) to the query grid.
   - In the **Query Design Toolbar**, click the **Totals** button (looks like a Greek Sigma `Σ`).
   - This adds a "Total" row in the query grid.

3. **Choose Grouping and Aggregation**:
   - For `ACTNUM`, leave the "Total" row as `Group By`.
   - For fields like `ACTNAME` or `ADD_DATE`, use aggregation functions like `First`, `Min`, or `Max`, depending on your requirements.

4. **Run the Query**:
   - Click **Run** to execute the query.

---

### **SQL Query for Access**
If you prefer to write the query directly in SQL View:
```sql
SELECT ACTNUM, 
       First(ACTNAME) AS FirstACTNAME, 
       First(CODE) AS FirstCODE, 
       First(ACCT_NAME) AS FirstACCT_NAME, 
       First(ACCT_TXT) AS FirstACCT_TXT, 
       First(ADD_DATE) AS FirstADD_DATE
FROM dbo_ACT_FILE
GROUP BY ACTNUM
ORDER BY ACTNUM;
```

---

### **Explanation**
1. **`GROUP BY`**:
   - Groups rows by the `ACTNUM` field, ensuring only one row per account number.

2. **`First()`**:
   - Picks the first value for fields like `ACTNAME`, `CODE`, etc., within each group. Replace with `Max()` or `Min()` if specific logic is needed.

3. **`ORDER BY`**:
   - Ensures the result is sorted by account number.

---

### **Result**
This query will return:
- One record for each unique `ACTNUM`.
- Additional fields (`ACTNAME`, `CODE`, etc.) populated with the first value found in the group.

```sql
UPDATE CustomerQueue

WHERE CustomerBillingContactsJson IS NOT NULL;
```

1. **`UPDATE CustomerQueue`**:

2. **`SET CustomerBillingContactsJson = '{}'`**:
   - Sets the value of the `CustomerBillingContactsJson` field to an empty JSON object `{}`.

3. **`WHERE CustomerBillingContactsJson IS NOT NULL`**:

{"name": "api_routes", "type": "code/csharp", "content": "// Routes for Consignee Account\napp.MapPost(\"/consignee/add\", async (ConsigneeAccountEntity consigneeAccount, ConsigneeAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    if (consigneeAccount == null) return Results.BadRequest(\"Consignee account cannot be null.\");\n    return await repository.AddConsigneeAccountAsync(consigneeAccount) \n        ? Results.Ok(\"Consignee account added successfully.\") \n        : Results.StatusCode(500, \"Failed to add consignee account.\");\n});\n\napp.MapGet(\"/consignee/{accountNumber}\", async (string accountNumber, ConsigneeAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    var account = await repository.GetConsigneeAccountByIdAsync(accountNumber);\n    return account != null ? Results.Ok(account) : Results.NotFound(\"Consignee account not found.\");\n});\n\napp.MapGet(\"/consignee/all\", async (ConsigneeAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    var accounts = await repository.GetConsigneeAccountsAsync();\n    return Results.Ok(accounts);\n});\n\napp.MapPut(\"/consignee/update\", async (ConsigneeAccountEntity consigneeAccount, ConsigneeAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    if (consigneeAccount == null) return Results.BadRequest(\"Consignee account cannot be null.\");\n    return await repository.UpdateConsigneeAccountAsync(consigneeAccount) \n        ? Results.Ok(\"Consignee account updated successfully.\") \n        : Results.NotFound(\"Consignee account not found.\");\n});\n\napp.MapDelete(\"/consignee/delete/{accountNumber}\", async (string accountNumber, ConsigneeAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    return await repository.DeleteConsigneeAccountAsync(accountNumber) \n        ? Results.Ok(\"Consignee account marked as deleted.\") \n        : Results.NotFound(\"Consignee account not found.\");\n});\n\n// Routes for Shipper Account\napp.MapPost(\"/shipper/add\", async (ShipperAccountEntity shipperAccount, ShipperAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    if (shipperAccount == null) return Results.BadRequest(\"Shipper account cannot be null.\");\n    return await repository.AddShipperAccountAsync(shipperAccount) \n        ? Results.Ok(\"Shipper account added successfully.\") \n        : Results.StatusCode(500, \"Failed to add shipper account.\");\n});\n\napp.MapGet(\"/shipper/{accountNumber}\", async (string accountNumber, ShipperAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    var account = await repository.GetShipperAccountByIdAsync(accountNumber);\n    return account != null ? Results.Ok(account) : Results.NotFound(\"Shipper account not found.\");\n});\n\napp.MapGet(\"/shipper/all\", async (ShipperAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    var accounts = await repository.GetShipperAccountsAsync();\n    return Results.Ok(accounts);\n});\n\napp.MapPut(\"/shipper/update\", async (ShipperAccountEntity shipperAccount, ShipperAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    if (shipperAccount == null) return Results.BadRequest(\"Shipper account cannot be null.\");\n    return await repository.UpdateShipperAccountAsync(shipperAccount) \n        ? Results.Ok(\"Shipper account updated successfully.\") \n        : Results.NotFound(\"Shipper account not found.\");\n});\n\napp.MapDelete(\"/shipper/delete/{accountNumber}\", async (string accountNumber, ShipperAccountRepository repository) =>\n{\n    return await repository.DeleteShipperAccountAsync(accountNumber) \n        ? Results.Ok(\"Shipper account marked as deleted.\") \n        : Results.NotFound(\"Shipper account not found.\");\n});"}

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Data.Repository;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Hubs;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Authenticate;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Customers;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Logging;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Onboarding;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Models.Entity;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Services.Authentication;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Services.Background;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Services.LogService;

using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.JwtBearer;

using Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authorization;

using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;

using Microsoft.OpenApi.Models;

using Serilog;



var builder = WebApplication.CreateBuilder(args);

Log.Logger = new LoggerConfiguration().WriteTo.Console().WriteTo.File("Logs/log-.txt", rollingInterval: RollingInterval.Day).CreateLogger();

builder.Host.UseSerilog();

builder.Services.AddDbContextFactory<PostOfficeContext>(options => options.UseSqlServer(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("PostOfficeConnection")));

builder.Services.AddDbContextFactory<LoggingContext>(options => options.UseSqlServer(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("LoggingConnection")));

builder.Services.AddDbContextFactory<OnboardingContext>(options => options.UseSqlServer(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("OnboardingConnection")));

builder.Services.AddDbContextFactory<ConnectionsContext>(options => options.UseSqlServer(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("SignalRConnection")));

builder.Services.AddDbContextFactory<CustomerContext>(options => options.UseSqlServer(builder.Configuration.GetConnectionString("ServerConnection")));



builder.Services.AddEndpointsApiExplorer();

builder.Services.AddSwaggerGen(c => { c.SwaggerDoc("v1", new OpenApiInfo { Title = "Logistics Mobile API", Version = "v1", Description = "API for managing logistics mobile applications" }); });

builder.Services.AddSignalR();

builder.Services.AddRazorPages();

builder.Services.AddHttpsRedirection(options => { options.RedirectStatusCode = StatusCodes.Status307TemporaryRedirect; options.HttpsPort = 443; });

builder.Services.AddSignalR(options => { options.KeepAliveInterval = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(10); options.ClientTimeoutInterval = TimeSpan.FromSeconds(20); });



builder.Services.AddTransient<ILoggingRepository, LoggingRepository>();

builder.Services.AddTransient<IPackageRepository, PackageRepository>();

builder.Services.AddTransient<IConnectionRepository, ConnectionRepository>();

builder.Services.AddTransient<IOnboardingRepository, OnboardingRepository>();

builder.Services.AddTransient<IAuthenticateService, AuthenticateService>();

builder.Services.AddTransient<ICustomerRepository, CustomerRepository>();



builder.Services.AddHostedService<PostOfficeService>();

builder.Services.AddAuthentication(o =>

{

    o.DefaultAuthenticateScheme = JwtBearerDefaults.AuthenticationScheme;

    o.DefaultChallengeScheme = JwtBearerDefaults.AuthenticationScheme;

}).AddJwtBearer(o => { 

    o.Audience = "your-api-id"; 

    o.RequireHttpsMetadata = false; 

});

var app = builder.Build(); app.UseSerilogRequestLogging(); using (var scope = app.Services.CreateScope())

{

    var repository = scope.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<IConnectionRepository>();

    try { await repository.TruncateTable(); Log.Information("Successfully truncated the connection table."); }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Failed to truncate the connection table."); }

}

app.UseMiddleware<RequestResponseLoggingMiddleware>();

if (app.Environment.IsDevelopment())

{

    app.UseDeveloperExceptionPage();

    app.UseSwagger();

    app.UseSwaggerUI(c => { c.SwaggerEndpoint("/swagger/v1/swagger.json", "Logistics Mobile API V1"); c.RoutePrefix = string.Empty; });

    app.Urls.Add("https://192.168.2.5:7072");

    app.Urls.Add("http://192.168.2.5:5072");

}

else { app.UseExceptionHandler("/Error"); }

app.MapRazorPages(); app.MapHub<SignalRDataRouter>("/data");

app.MapHub<SignalRChatRouter>("/audio");

app.UseRouting();

app.UseStaticFiles();

app.UseAuthentication();

app.UseAuthorization();



app.MapPost("/authenticate/", [AllowAnonymous] async (IAuthenticateService authenticateService, string apiKey, string appGUID) =>

{

    if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(apiKey))

    {

        Log.Warning("Authentication failed: API Key is empty.");

        return Results.Text("API Key is empty.");

    }

    try

    {

        var result = await authenticateService.LoginAsync(apiKey, appGUID);

        if (result.Succeeded) { Log.Information("User authenticated successfully."); return Results.Ok(result); }

        else

        {

            Log.Warning("Authentication failed for API Key {ApiKey}.", apiKey);

            return Results.BadRequest(result);

        }

    }

    catch (Exception ex)

    {

        Log.Error(ex, "Error during authentication.");

        return Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

});





app.MapPost("/customers/add/", async (CustomerEntity customer, ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    if (customer == null) return Results.BadRequest("Customer cannot be null.");

    try

    {

        var result = await customerRepository.AddCustomerAsync(customer);

        return result ? Results.Ok("Customer added successfully.") : Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while adding a customer."); return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

app.MapGet("/customers/", async ( ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    try

    {

        var customer = await customerRepository.GetCustomers();

        return customer != null ? Results.Ok(customer) : Results.NotFound("Customer not found.");

    }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error retrieving customer by ID."); return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

app.MapGet("/customers/get/{id:guid}", async (Guid id, ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    if (id == Guid.Empty) return Results.BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");

    try { var customer = await customerRepository.GetCustomerByIdAsync(id); 

        return customer != null ? Results.Ok(customer) : Results.NotFound("Customer not found."); }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error retrieving customer by ID."); return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

app.MapPut("/customers/update/", async (CustomerEntity customer, ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    if (customer == null) return Results.BadRequest("Customer cannot be null.");

    try { var result = await customerRepository.UpdateCustomerAsync(customer); 

        return result ? Results.Ok("Customer updated successfully.") : Results.NotFound("Customer not found."); }

    catch (InvalidOperationException ex) { Log.Warning(ex, "Concurrency conflict occurred."); 

        return Results.Conflict(ex.Message); }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error updating customer."); 

        return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

app.MapPut("/customers/{id:guid}/contacts/update", async (Guid id, string billingContactsJson, ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    if (id == Guid.Empty) return Results.BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");

    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(billingContactsJson)) return Results.BadRequest("Billing contacts JSON cannot be empty.");

    try { var result = await customerRepository.UpdateCustomerContactsAsync(id, billingContactsJson); return result ? Results.Ok("Customer contacts updated successfully.") : Results.NotFound("Customer not found."); }

    catch (InvalidOperationException ex) { Log.Warning(ex, "Concurrency conflict occurred while updating contacts."); return Results.Conflict(ex.Message); }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error updating customer contacts."); return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

app.MapDelete("/customers/delete/{id:guid}", async (Guid id, ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    if (id == Guid.Empty) return Results.BadRequest("Customer ID cannot be empty.");

    try { var result = await customerRepository.DeleteCustomerAsync(id); return result ? Results.Ok("Customer deleted successfully.") : Results.NotFound("Customer not found."); }

    catch (InvalidOperationException ex) { Log.Warning(ex, "Concurrency conflict occurred while deleting customer."); return Results.Conflict(ex.Message); }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error deleting customer."); return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

app.MapGet("/customers/search/accountnumber", async (string accountNumber, ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) return Results.BadRequest("Account number cannot be null or empty.");

    try { var customer = await customerRepository.GetCustomerByAccountNumberAsync(accountNumber); return customer != null ? Results.Ok(customer) : Results.NotFound("Customer not found."); }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error searching customer by account number."); return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

app.MapGet("/customers/search/accountname", async (string accountName, ICustomerRepository customerRepository) =>

{

    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountName)) return Results.BadRequest("Account name cannot be null or empty.");

    try { var customers = await customerRepository.SearchCustomersByAccountNameAsync(accountName); return Results.Ok(customers); }

    catch (Exception ex) { Log.Error(ex, "Error searching customers by account name."); return Results.StatusCode(500); }

});

   Create a method in each repository that takes a search term and looks up records in multiple fields (e.g., `AccountNumber`, `AccountName`, `Address1`, etc.).

### **Update Repository Logic**

#### ConsigneeRepository
Add a new method:
```csharp
public async Task<IEnumerable<ConsigneeEntity>> SearchConsigneeAsync(string searchTerm)
{
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(searchTerm)) throw new ArgumentException("Search term cannot be null or empty.", nameof(searchTerm));

    using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();
    return await context.Set<ConsigneeEntity>()
        .Where(c =>
            EF.Functions.Like(c.AccountNumber, $"%{searchTerm}%") ||
            EF.Functions.Like(c.AccountName, $"%{searchTerm}%") ||
            EF.Functions.Like(c.Address1, $"%{searchTerm}%") ||
            EF.Functions.Like(c.City, $"%{searchTerm}%"))
        .ToListAsync();
}
```

#### ShipperRepository
Add a similar method:
```csharp
public async Task<IEnumerable<ShipperEntity>> SearchShipperAsync(string searchTerm)
{
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(searchTerm)) throw new ArgumentException("Search term cannot be null or empty.", nameof(searchTerm));

    using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();
    return await context.Set<ShipperEntity>()
        .Where(s =>
            EF.Functions.Like(s.AccountNumber, $"%{searchTerm}%") ||
            EF.Functions.Like(s.AccountName, $"%{searchTerm}%") ||
            EF.Functions.Like(s.Address1, $"%{searchTerm}%") ||
            EF.Functions.Like(s.City, $"%{searchTerm}%"))
        .ToListAsync();
}
```

---

### **Add Routes in `Program.cs`**

#### For Consignee
Add this route:
```csharp
app.MapGet("/consignee/search", async (string searchTerm, ConsigneeRepository repository) =>
{
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(searchTerm)) return Results.BadRequest("Search term cannot be null or empty.");
    try
    {
        var results = await repository.SearchConsigneeAsync(searchTerm);
        return results.Any() ? Results.Ok(results) : Results.NotFound("No matching consignees found.");
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while searching consignees.");
        return Results.StatusCode(500);
    }
});
```

#### For Shipper
Add this route:

```csharp
{
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(searchTerm)) return Results.BadRequest("Search term cannot be null or empty.");

        return Results.StatusCode(500);

});
```

## ASSISTANT
You're aiming for a **responsive autocomplete** search in a WinForms application without overloading the API with requests. Here's how you can approach the problem using **SignalR**, caching strategies, and smarter API design:

---

### **1. SignalR for Real-Time Search**
SignalR is a great option to handle real-time communication for autocomplete:
- **How It Works**:
  - The client establishes a SignalR connection to the server.
  - On every user input (e.g., typing a letter), the client sends the search query to the server via SignalR.
  - The server processes the query and returns results in real time, avoiding multiple HTTP API calls.

#### Implementation
1. **Setup SignalR Hub**:
   - Add a `SearchHub` on the server:
     ```csharp
     public class SearchHub : Hub
     {
         private readonly SearchService _searchService;

         public SearchHub(SearchService searchService)
         {
             _searchService = searchService;
         }

         public async Task<List<SearchResult>> Search(string query)
         {
             if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(query)) return new List<SearchResult>();
             return await _searchService.SearchAllAsync(query);
         }
     }
     ```

2. **WinForms SignalR Client**:
   - Use the SignalR client library (`Microsoft.AspNetCore.SignalR.Client`) in your WinForms application.
   - On user input, call the `Search` method on the SignalR hub:
     ```csharp
     private async void SearchBox_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
     {
         var query = searchBox.Text;
         if (query.Length < 2) return; // Avoid small inputs
         var results = await _hubConnection.InvokeAsync<List<SearchResult>>("Search", query);
         autocompleteBox.DataSource = results.Select(r => r.Name).ToList();
     }
     ```

---

### **2. Smart Caching with Mark1 and Samson Principles**
- Instead of caching everything, use **harmonic caching** to store only high-probability data based on:
  - Frequently searched terms.
  - Recently searched terms.
  - Predictive models for likely inputs.

#### Strategy
1. **Harmonic Cache Design**:
   - Assign a "resonance score" to cached items based on their probability of reuse.
   - Use an **LRU (Least Recently Used)** eviction policy weighted by the score.

2. **Dynamic Updates**:
   - The server-side `SearchService` can keep a local, in-memory cache for high-resonance terms.
   - Use Samson’s feedback stabilization to adjust cache contents dynamically.

---

### **3. Use Telerik AutocompleteBox**
- Telerik's **RadAutoCompleteBox** supports remote data sources and can work with SignalR.
- **Configuration**:
  1. Set up the `RadAutoCompleteBox` in WinForms.
  2. Use the `AutoCompleteMode.SuggestAppend` to display suggestions.
  3. Hook it to the SignalR client for fetching suggestions dynamically.

```csharp
private async void AutoCompleteBox_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    var query = autoCompleteBox.Text;
    if (query.Length < 2) return;

    var results = await _hubConnection.InvokeAsync<List<SearchResult>>("Search", query);
    autoCompleteBox.AutoCompleteCustomSource.Clear();
    autoCompleteBox.AutoCompleteCustomSource.AddRange(results.Select(r => r.Name).ToArray());
}
```

---

### **4. Use a Hybrid Strategy**
- **First Few Characters**:
  - Fetch results from a **server-side cache** using SignalR.
- **Longer Queries**:
  - Query the database directly for more refined results.
- **Timeout Handling**:
  - If a SignalR call is delayed, show cached results and update asynchronously when the response arrives.

---

### **5. Tools/NuGet to Consider**
- **SignalR Client**: `Microsoft.AspNetCore.SignalR.Client`
- **LRU Cache**: `Microsoft.Extensions.Caching.Memory`
- **Telerik Controls**: Ensure you have the WinForms Telerik package installed.

---

### **Advantages**
- **Real-Time Updates**: SignalR avoids repetitive API calls.
- **Smarter Cache**: Mark1 and Samson principles ensure efficient caching without overloading memory.
- **Responsive UI**: Telerik’s autocomplete box with remote data sources provides a seamless user experience.

        private void manageIncomingToolStripMenuItem_Click(object sender, EventArgs e)

        {

            if (OpenForms.OfType<RoutingDashboard>().Count() == 0)

            {

                var routingForm = Program.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<RoutingDashboard>();

                routingForm.Show();

            }

        }

                var routingForm = Program.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<BillingDashboard>();

                routingForm.Show();

            }

        }



        private void mainSettings_Click(object sender, EventArgs e)

        {

            if (OpenForms.OfType<RoutingDashboard>().Count() == 0)

            {

                var routingForm = Program.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<SettingsDashboard>();

                routingForm.Show();

            }

        }

    }

}




## ASSISTANT
You can modify your button click handlers to open three different dashboards by using a `ToolStripDropDownButton` or splitting the actions into a menu dropdown. Here's how you can achieve this:

### Add Buttons for Three Dashboards
Add three specific buttons or menu items under the relevant `ToolStripDropDownButton`.

For example:
```csharp
// Modify the mainToolStrip to include three new buttons
mainToolStrip.Items.AddRange(new System.Windows.Forms.ToolStripItem[] {
    new ToolStripButton("Dashboard 1", null, Dashboard1_Click),
    new ToolStripButton("Dashboard 2", null, Dashboard2_Click),
    new ToolStripButton("Dashboard 3", null, Dashboard3_Click),
});
```

### Implement Click Handlers
Add the corresponding methods to handle button clicks:
```csharp
private void Dashboard1_Click(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    OpenDashboard<Dashboard1>();
}

private void Dashboard2_Click(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    OpenDashboard<Dashboard2>();
}

private void Dashboard3_Click(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    OpenDashboard<Dashboard3>();
}

// Helper method to ensure a single instance of each dashboard
private void OpenDashboard<T>() where T : Form
{
    var existingForm = Application.OpenForms.OfType<T>().FirstOrDefault();
    if (existingForm != null)
    {
        existingForm.BringToFront();
    }
    else
    {
        var dashboardForm = Program.ServiceProvider.GetRequiredService<T>();
        dashboardForm.Show();
    }
}
```

### Update the Form Designer
Ensure the `ToolStripButton` entries are updated in `InitializeComponent` to reflect the additional buttons.

This implementation ensures each dashboard opens as a singleton and avoids duplicates. Adjust the form names and methods to match your actual dashboard class names. Let me know if you need more details!

## ASSISTANT
To convert the Access table data into JSON format and update the SQL Server table, you can use VBA in Access to generate the JSON strings, then update the SQL Server table via an update query.

---

### **Steps to Generate JSON in Access**

1. **Create a VBA Function to Generate JSON**:
   Use the VBA `JsonConverter` library or the built-in `Dictionary` object to serialize rows into JSON.

#### Install the JSON Library
- Download the `JsonConverter.bas` file from [here](https://github.com/VBA-tools/VBA-JSON) and import it into your Access database.

#### VBA Code to Generate JSON
```vba
Public Function GenerateBillingJson(AccountNumber As String, AccountName As String, AccountStore As String, _
                                    AccountAddress1 As String, AccountAddress2 As String, AccountAddress3 As String, _
                                    AccountCity As String, AccountState As String, AccountZip As String, _
                                    AccountPhone As String, AccountPhone2 As String, AccountFax As String, _
                                    AccountEmail As String, AccountContactName As String, AccountContactTitle As String, _
                                    AccountContactPhone As String, AccountContactText As String, _
                                    AccountContactEmail As String, AccountContactCell As String, _
                                    AccountContactFax As String) As String
    Dim dict As Object
    Set dict = CreateObject("Scripting.Dictionary")
    
    dict("accountNumber") = AccountNumber
    dict("accountName") = AccountName
    dict("accountStore") = AccountStore
    dict("accountAddress1") = AccountAddress1
    dict("accountAddress2") = AccountAddress2
    dict("accountAddress3") = AccountAddress3
    dict("accountCity") = AccountCity
    dict("accountState") = AccountState
    dict("accountZip") = AccountZip
    dict("accountPhone") = AccountPhone
    dict("accountPhone2") = AccountPhone2
    dict("accountFax") = AccountFax
    dict("accountEmail") = AccountEmail
    dict("accountContactName") = AccountContactName
    dict("accountContactTitle") = AccountContactTitle
    dict("accountContactPhone") = AccountContactPhone
    dict("accountContactText") = AccountContactText
    dict("accountContactEmail") = AccountContactEmail
    dict("accountContactCell") = AccountContactCell
    dict("accountContactFax") = AccountContactFax

    GenerateBillingJson = JsonConverter.ConvertToJson(dict)
End Function
```

---

2. **Update the Access Table with JSON**:
   Add a new column (e.g., `BillingJson`) to the Access table to store the generated JSON.

#### Create an Update Query
```sql
UPDATE customer_billing
SET BillingJson = GenerateBillingJson([AccountNumber], [AccountName], [AccountStore], [AccountAddress1], 
[AccountAddress2], [AccountAddress3], [AccountCity], [AccountState], [AccountZip], [AccountPhone], 
[AccountPhone2], [AccountFax], [AccountEmail], [AccountContactName], [AccountContactTitle], 
[AccountContactPhone], [AccountContactText], [AccountContactEmail], [AccountContactCell], 
[AccountContactFax]);
```

---

3. **Export JSON to SQL Server**:
Use an ODBC connection or a linked table in Access to update the SQL Server table.

#### Update SQL Table with JSON
Run an Access query to update the SQL Server table:
```sql
UPDATE SQLServerTable
SET BillingContactsJson = BillingJson
FROM AccessTable
WHERE SQLServerTable.AccountNumber = AccessTable.AccountNumber;
```

---

### **Summary**
1. **Generate JSON** using the `GenerateBillingJson` VBA function.
2. **Store JSON** in a temporary Access column (`BillingJson`).
3. **Update SQL Server** by transferring the JSON from Access to the SQL Server `BillingContactsJson` column.
