## ASSISTANT
If you want to handcraft the JSON in Access directly using a query in the **Expression Builder** or **SQL View**, you can construct it using string concatenation with table fields.

---

### **Example Query**
Here’s how you can build the JSON using an **Update Query** in Access:

```sql
UPDATE customer_billing
SET BillingJson = 
'{' +
    '"accountNumber":"' + Nz([AccountNumber], '') + '",' +
    '"accountName":"' + Nz([AccountName], '') + '",' +
    '"accountStore":"' + Nz([AccountStore], '') + '",' +
    '"accountAddress1":"' + Nz([AccountAddress1], '') + '",' +
    '"accountAddress2":"' + Nz([AccountAddress2], '') + '",' +
    '"accountAddress3":"' + Nz([AccountAddress3], '') + '",' +
    '"accountCity":"' + Nz([AccountCity], '') + '",' +
    '"accountState":"' + Nz([AccountState], '') + '",' +
    '"accountZip":"' + Nz([AccountZip], '') + '",' +
    '"accountPhone":"' + Nz([AccountPhone], '') + '",' +
    '"accountPhone2":"' + Nz([AccountPhone2], '') + '",' +
    '"accountFax":"' + Nz([AccountFax], '') + '",' +
    '"accountEmail":"' + Nz([AccountEmail], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactName":"' + Nz([AccountContactName], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactTitle":"' + Nz([AccountContactTitle], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactPhone":"' + Nz([AccountContactPhone], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactText":"' + Nz([AccountContactText], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactEmail":"' + Nz([AccountContactEmail], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactCell":"' + Nz([AccountContactCell], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactFax":"' + Nz([AccountContactFax], '') + '"' +
'}';
```

---

### **How It Works**
1. **`Nz([FieldName], '')`**:
   - Ensures null fields are replaced with empty strings to avoid errors in concatenation.
   
2. **String Concatenation**:
   - Each field value is wrapped in quotes and concatenated with JSON syntax.

3. **Result**:
   - The JSON string is stored in the `BillingJson` column for each row.

---

### **Steps to Use**
1. Open the **Query Design** tool in Access.
2. Switch to **SQL View**.
3. Paste the query above.
4. Run the query to populate the `BillingJson` field.

---

To fix and dynamically generate the JSON from `customer_billing` data and update the `dbo_customer_account.billing_address_json` field, you can modify your SQL query as follows:

---

### **Fixed SQL Query**
```sql
UPDATE dbo_customer_account
INNER JOIN customer_billing 
ON dbo_customer_account.account_number = customer_billing.AccountNumber
SET dbo_customer_account.billing_address_json = 
'{' +
    '"accountNumber":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountNumber], '') + '",' +
    '"accountName":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountName], '') + '",' +
    '"accountStore":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountStore], '') + '",' +
    '"accountAddress1":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountAddress1], '') + '",' +
    '"accountAddress2":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountAddress2], '') + '",' +
    '"accountAddress3":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountAddress3], '') + '",' +
    '"accountCity":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountCity], '') + '",' +
    '"accountState":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountState], '') + '",' +
    '"accountZip":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountZip], '') + '",' +
    '"accountPhone":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountPhone], '') + '",' +
    '"accountPhone2":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountPhone2], '') + '",' +
    '"accountFax":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountFax], '') + '",' +
    '"accountEmail":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountEmail], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactName":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountContactName], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactTitle":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountContactTitle], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactPhone":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountContactPhone], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactText":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountContactText], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactEmail":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountContactEmail], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactCell":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountContactCell], '') + '",' +
    '"accountContactFax":"' + Nz([customer_billing].[AccountContactFax], '') + '"' +
'}';
```

---

### **Explanation**
1. **`Nz()` Function**:
   - Handles null values gracefully by replacing them with empty strings (`''`).

2. **String Concatenation**:
   - Combines field values into a JSON structure.
   - Includes field names in double quotes and values in single quotes.

3. **Dynamic JSON Generation**:
   - Uses `customer_billing` fields to dynamically build the JSON string.

4. **Join Logic**:
   - Ensures the `INNER JOIN` matches rows based on `account_number`.

---

### **Steps to Run**
1. Open Access and go to **Query Design**.
2. Switch to **SQL View**.
3. Paste the query and run it.

## ASSISTANT
To create a migration in Entity Framework Core using the **Package Manager Console (PMC)** in Visual Studio, use the following command:

---

### **Command**
```powershell
Add-Migration MigrationName -Context YourDbContext
```

---

### **Parameters**
- **`MigrationName`**: Replace with a descriptive name for your migration, e.g., `InitialCreate` or `AddBillingJson`.
- **`-Context YourDbContext`**: Specify the DbContext class to use if your project has multiple DbContexts.

---

### **Example**
If your DbContext is named `CustomerContext` and you are adding a `BillingJson` column, the command might look like:
```powershell
Add-Migration AddBillingJson -Context CustomerContext
```

---

### **After Creating the Migration**
Apply the migration to the database:
```powershell
Update-Database -Context CustomerContext
```

---

### **Helpful PMC Commands**
1. **List Migrations**:
   ```powershell
   Get-Migrations -Context CustomerContext
   ```
2. **Remove the Last Migration**:
   ```powershell
   Remove-Migration -Context CustomerContext
   ```

## ASSISTANT
To handle row selection in the `RadGridView` and populate the `valCustomer` fields with concatenated address and contact information, we can use the `SelectionChanged` event. The concatenated values can either be derived dynamically from the existing columns or from hidden columns if the data isn't readily available.

Here's the implementation:

---

### **Steps to Implement**

1. **Handle Row Selection in `RadGridView`**
   Attach an event handler to the `SelectionChanged` event of the `RadGridView`.

2. **Concatenate Address and Contact**
   Dynamically concatenate the values from the `RadGridView`'s selected row or from hidden fields if needed.

3. **Populate the `valCustomer` Fields**
   Set the values of `valCustomerAccountBillingAddress` and `valCustomerBillingMainContact`.

---

### **Code Implementation**

```csharp
public partial class CustomerDashboard : Telerik.WinControls.UI.RadForm
{
    public CustomerDashboard()
    {
        InitializeComponent();

        // Attach event handler for row selection
        radGridView1.SelectionChanged += RadGridView1_SelectionChanged;
    }

    private void RadGridView1_SelectionChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
    {
        // Get the selected row
        var selectedRow = radGridView1.CurrentRow;
        if (selectedRow == null) return;

        // Extract data from the selected row
        var accountName = selectedRow.Cells["AccountName"].Value?.ToString();
        var address1 = selectedRow.Cells["AccountAddress1"]?.Value?.ToString();
        var address2 = selectedRow.Cells["AccountAddress2"]?.Value?.ToString();
        var city = selectedRow.Cells["AccountCity"]?.Value?.ToString();
        var state = selectedRow.Cells["AccountState"]?.Value?.ToString();
        var zip = selectedRow.Cells["AccountZip"]?.Value?.ToString();
        var mainContact = selectedRow.Cells["AccountContactName"]?.Value?.ToString();
        var contactPhone = selectedRow.Cells["AccountContactPhone"]?.Value?.ToString();
        var contactEmail = selectedRow.Cells["AccountContactEmail"]?.Value?.ToString();

        // Concatenate address
        var fullAddress = $"{address1}, {address2}, {city}, {state} {zip}";

        // Concatenate contact
        var fullContact = $"{mainContact}\nPhone: {contactPhone}\nEmail: {contactEmail}";

        // Populate the fields
        valCustomerAccountBillingAddress.Text = fullAddress;
        valCustomerBillingMainContact.Text = fullContact;

        // Optionally set AccountName
        valCustomerAccountName.Text = accountName;
    }
}
```

---

### **Additional Considerations**
1. **Hidden Columns**:
   If the address and contact information are not visible in the `RadGridView`, you can add hidden columns to hold the data:
   ```csharp
   gridViewTextBoxColumn5.IsVisible = false; // For hidden columns
   ```

2. **Validation**:
   Ensure `null` checks are in place to avoid exceptions from missing data.

3. **Data Binding**:
   If the `RadGridView` is data-bound, ensure all required fields are part of the data source.

4. **UI Updates**:
   The concatenated fields will update dynamically as the selection changes.

---

This approach maintains simplicity while dynamically filling the fields based on the current row selection. Let me know if you'd like further enhancements!

using System.Windows.Forms;

using System.Threading.Tasks;

using Telerik.WinControls;

using Telerik.WinControls.UI;

using Logistics.Application.Models.customers;

using Logistics.Application.Persistance.Interfaces;



namespace Logistics.GUI.Forms.Customer

{

    public partial class CustomerDashboard : RadForm

    {

        private readonly ICustomerRepository _customerRepository;



        public CustomerDashboard(ICustomerRepository customerRepository)

        {

            _customerRepository = customerRepository ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customerRepository));

            InitializeComponent();



            // Disable automatic row sizing

            radGridView1.AutoSizeRows = false;





            // Attach the RowFormatting event

            radGridView1.RowFormatting += RadGridView1_RowFormatting;



            // Attach the CellEndEdit event for updates

            radGridView1.CellEndEdit += RadGridView1_CellEndEdit;



            // Load data when the form initializes

            Load += CustomerDashboard_Load;

        }



        // Event handler for formatting rows dynamically

        private void RadGridView1_RowFormatting(object sender, RowFormattingEventArgs e)

        {

           

        }



        // Load customer data into the grid on form load

        private async void CustomerDashboard_Load(object sender, EventArgs e)

        {

            try

            {

                var customers = await _customerRepository.GetAllCustomersAsync();

                radGridView1.DataSource = customers;

            }

            catch (Exception ex)

            {

                MessageBox.Show($"Error loading customers: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);

            }

        }



        // Handle updates when a cell edit ends

        private async void RadGridView1_CellEndEdit(object sender, GridViewCellEventArgs e)

        {

            try

            {

                var editedCustomer = (CustomerEntity)e.Row.DataBoundItem;

                var success = await _customerRepository.UpdateCustomerAsync(editedCustomer);



                if (!success)

                {

                    MessageBox.Show("Failed to update customer.", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);

                }

            }

            catch (Exception ex)

            {

                MessageBox.Show($"Error updating customer: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);

            }

        }

    }

}


## ASSISTANT
To refactor the code as requested:

1. **Move the dataset retrieval to a class-level variable**:
   - This allows the dataset to persist for lookups.

2. **Use the account number to find the record**:
   - When a row is selected in the `RadGridView`, retrieve the corresponding record using the account number and unpack the `customer_address_json` field.

3. **Unpack `customer_address_json` using the `CustomerContactEntity`**:
   - Deserialize the JSON and populate the appropriate fields in the form.

---

### **Refactored Code**

```csharp
using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Text.Json;
using System.Windows.Forms;
using Telerik.WinControls.UI;
using Logistics.Application.Models.customers;
using Logistics.Application.Persistance.Interfaces;

namespace Logistics.GUI.Forms.Customer
{
    public partial class CustomerDashboard : RadForm
    {
        private readonly ICustomerRepository _customerRepository;
        private List<CustomerEntity> _customerData; // Class-level dataset

        public CustomerDashboard(ICustomerRepository customerRepository)
        {
            _customerRepository = customerRepository ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customerRepository));
            InitializeComponent();

            // Disable automatic row sizing
            radGridView1.AutoSizeRows = false;

            // Attach events
            radGridView1.RowFormatting += RadGridView1_RowFormatting;
            radGridView1.CellEndEdit += RadGridView1_CellEndEdit;
            radGridView1.SelectionChanged += RadGridView1_SelectionChanged;

            // Load data when the form initializes
            Load += CustomerDashboard_Load;
        }

        // Event handler for formatting rows dynamically
        private void RadGridView1_RowFormatting(object sender, RowFormattingEventArgs e)
        {
            // Implement custom row formatting logic if needed
        }

        // Load customer data into the grid on form load
        private async void CustomerDashboard_Load(object sender, EventArgs e)
        {
            try
            {
                _customerData = (await _customerRepository.GetAllCustomersAsync()).ToList();
                radGridView1.DataSource = _customerData;
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                MessageBox.Show($"Error loading customers: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
            }
        }

        // Handle updates when a cell edit ends
        private async void RadGridView1_CellEndEdit(object sender, GridViewCellEventArgs e)
        {
            try
            {
                var editedCustomer = (CustomerEntity)e.Row.DataBoundItem;
                var success = await _customerRepository.UpdateCustomerAsync(editedCustomer);

                if (!success)
                {
                    MessageBox.Show("Failed to update customer.", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
                }
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                MessageBox.Show($"Error updating customer: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
            }
        }

        // Handle row selection to populate customer details
        private void RadGridView1_SelectionChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
        {
            var selectedRow = radGridView1.CurrentRow;
            if (selectedRow == null) return;

            var accountNumber = selectedRow.Cells["AccountNumber"].Value?.ToString();
            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) return;

            // Find the corresponding record in the dataset
            var selectedCustomer = _customerData.FirstOrDefault(c => c.AccountNumber == accountNumber);
            if (selectedCustomer == null) return;

            // Unpack the customer_address_json field
            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(selectedCustomer.CustomerBillingContactsJson))
            {
                try
                {
                    var contacts = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<List<CustomerContactEntity>>(selectedCustomer.CustomerBillingContactsJson);
                    if (contacts != null && contacts.Any())
                    {
                        // Example: Concatenate address and contact details
                        var mainAddress = $"{contacts[0].AccountAddress1}, {contacts[0].AccountCity}, {contacts[0].AccountState} {contacts[0].AccountZip}";
                        var mainContact = $"{contacts[0].AccountContactName}\nPhone: {contacts[0].AccountContactPhone}\nEmail: {contacts[0].AccountContactEmail}";

                        // Populate the form fields
                        valCustomerAccountBillingAddress.Text = mainAddress;
                        valCustomerBillingMainContact.Text = mainContact;
                        valCustomerAccountName.Text = selectedCustomer.AccountName;
                    }
                }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    MessageBox.Show($"Error unpacking customer JSON: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
                }
            }
        }
    }
}
```

---

### **Key Changes**
1. **Added `_customerData`**:
   - Holds the dataset at the class level for lookups.

2. **Modified `CustomerDashboard_Load`**:
   - Populates `_customerData` for later use.

3. **Implemented `RadGridView1_SelectionChanged`**:
   - Uses the account number to find the record in `_customerData`.

## ASSISTANT
The error indicates two distinct issues:

1. **Metadata file missing**:
   - This often occurs when a dependent project fails to build, preventing the generation of its `.dll` file.

2. **Package compatibility**:
   - The `UI.for.WinForms.AllControls.Net80` package requires `.NET 8.0` with the `windows` target framework moniker (TFM), i.e., `net8.0-windows`.

---

### **Steps to Resolve**

#### **1. Fix Missing Metadata File Issue**
This occurs when a referenced project isn't building successfully. Here's how to address it:
1. **Clean and Rebuild the Solution**:
   - Right-click the solution in Solution Explorer.
   - Select **Clean Solution**.
   - Then select **Rebuild Solution**.

2. **Check for Build Errors**:
   - Inspect the `Output` window or the `Error List` to identify build issues in the dependent project (`Logistics.Application` in this case).

3. **Ensure Project References Are Correct**:
   - Open the **References** node under `Logistics.Winform` in Solution Explorer.
   - Ensure that `Logistics.Application` is listed as a reference.
   - If it's missing, add it back:
     - Right-click **References** > **Add Reference** > **Projects** tab > Select `Logistics.Application`.

4. **Check Output Paths**:
   - Ensure the `Output Path` for `Logistics.Application` matches the path expected by `Logistics.Winform`:
     - Right-click `Logistics.Application` > **Properties** > **Build** tab.
     - Ensure the `Output Path` is set to `bin\Debug\net8.0` or appropriate for your configuration.

---

#### **2. Fix Package Compatibility Issue**
The Telerik `UI.for.WinForms.AllControls.Net80` package supports `net8.0-windows`, not just `net8.0`. Update your project's target framework to `net8.0-windows`:

1. **Update the Target Framework**:
   - Open the `.csproj` file of `Logistics.Winform`.
   - Update the `<TargetFramework>` to:
     ```xml
     <TargetFramework>net8.0-windows</TargetFramework>
     ```

2. **Ensure the Windows Desktop Workload is Installed**:
   - Open Visual Studio Installer.
   - Under **Modify** for your installation, ensure the **Windows Forms Development** workload is installed.

3. **Restore NuGet Packages**:
   - Open the **Package Manager Console** (Tools > NuGet Package Manager > Package Manager Console).
   - Run:
     ```powershell
     dotnet restore
     ```

4. **Clean and Rebuild**:
   - Repeat the clean and rebuild steps outlined earlier.

---

### **Common Causes**
- **TFM Mismatch**: The project references a library/package that requires a specific TFM (`net8.0-windows` in this case).
- **Dependent Project Fails to Build**: Causes metadata errors since the `.dll` file isn't generated.

---

## ASSISTANT
The error indicates a **`System.Data.SqlTypes.SqlNullValueException`** is being thrown because a column in your query result is `NULL`, and the application code attempts to access it without checking for null.

---

### **Cause**
1. A `NULL` value is being returned from one or more database fields (`customer_account`) for columns like `account_full_name`, `account_name`, or others.
2. The code is attempting to read the value directly using methods like `.GetString()` without checking for `NULL`.

---

### **Fix**
#### 1. **Add Null Checks in EF Core Query**
Modify the projection to handle potential `NULL` values in the database. For example:
```csharp
var customers = await _context.Customers
    .Select(c => new CustomerEntity
    {
        Id = c.Id,
        AccountFullName = c.AccountFullName ?? string.Empty, // Handle NULL
        AccountName = c.AccountName ?? string.Empty,         // Handle NULL
        AccountNumber = c.AccountNumber ?? string.Empty,     // Handle NULL
        AccountText = c.AccountText ?? string.Empty,         // Handle NULL
        BillingContactsJson = c.BillingContactsJson ?? string.Empty,
        BillingAddressJson = c.BillingAddressJson ?? string.Empty,
        CustomerType = c.CustomerType ?? string.Empty,
        DateCreated = c.DateCreated ?? DateTime.MinValue,     // Handle NULL dates
        RowVersion = c.RowVersion ?? Array.Empty<byte>()      // Handle NULL binary
    })
    .ToListAsync();
```

---

#### 2. **Fix at the Database Level**
Ensure the database columns have either:
1. Default values defined (e.g., `DEFAULT ''` for strings).
2. Non-`NULL` constraints if appropriate.

Run the following script to identify which columns allow `NULL` values and correct them:

```sql
SELECT COLUMN_NAME, IS_NULLABLE, DATA_TYPE
FROM INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS
WHERE TABLE_NAME = 'customer_account';
```

To update columns to `NOT NULL` with default values:
```sql
ALTER TABLE customer_account
ALTER COLUMN account_full_name NVARCHAR(255) NOT NULL;
```

To set default values:
```sql
ALTER TABLE customer_account
ADD CONSTRAINT DF_account_full_name DEFAULT '' FOR account_full_name;
```

---

#### 3. **Debugging Strategy**
1. Check the log for the exact column(s) causing the exception.
2. Temporarily modify your SQL query or repository method to pinpoint the `NULL` values:
```csharp
var customers = await _context.Customers
    .Where(c => c.AccountFullName == null || c.AccountName == null)
    .ToListAsync();
```

---

#### 4. **Optional Logging**
Log the rows causing issues before exceptions are thrown:
```csharp
var query = _context.Customers.ToList();
foreach (var row in query)
{
    if (row.AccountFullName == null)
    {
        Log.Warning("AccountFullName is NULL for customer ID: {Id}", row.Id);
    }
}
```

---

### **Summary**
1. **Code Fix**: Update EF Core projections to handle `NULL` values gracefully.
2. **Database Fix**: Ensure columns have default values or `NOT NULL` constraints.
3. **Debug**: Identify problematic rows and fix the data directly.

## USER
whats a good font for a form in winform

## ASSISTANT
A good font for a WinForms application depends on readability, aesthetics, and compatibility across systems. Here are some recommendations:

---

### **General Recommendations**
1. **Segoe UI** (Default Windows Font)
   - **Why**: Modern, clean, and highly readable. It’s the default UI font in Windows.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Segoe UI", 9F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```
   - **Sizes**:
     - Use `9pt` for general UI text.
     - Use `12pt` for headers or larger labels.

2. **Calibri**
   - **Why**: Smooth and professional, commonly used in office environments.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Calibri", 10F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```

3. **Arial**
   - **Why**: Universally available and easy to read, though less modern than Segoe UI.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Arial", 9F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```

4. **Verdana**
   - **Why**: Good for smaller text, as it’s wider and more legible.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Verdana", 9F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```

---

### **For a Modern Look**
1. **Roboto** (Google Font)
   - **Why**: Designed for readability on screens, widely used in modern apps.
   - **Installation**: Download and install it first, then set it in your app.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Roboto", 9F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```

2. **Open Sans** (Google Font)
   - **Why**: Clean and neutral, often used in web apps and modern UIs.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Open Sans", 9F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```

---

### **For a Stylish Look**
1. **Tahoma**
   - **Why**: Compact and slightly more stylized than Arial or Segoe UI.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Tahoma", 10F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```

2. **Century Gothic**
   - **Why**: Sleek and modern, with a bit of flair.
   - **Example**:
     ```csharp
     this.Font = new Font("Century Gothic", 10F, FontStyle.Regular);
     ```

---

### **Practical Tips**
- **Consistency**: Use the same font family across the entire application for a cohesive look.
- **Size**: Use `9pt` or `10pt` for regular text and slightly larger for headers.
- **Fallbacks**: Always test your font to ensure it’s available on target systems.

---

Let me know if you'd like help applying a specific font across your forms!

## ASSISTANT
The error indicates that the JSON being deserialized is not a list (array) of `CustomerContactEntity`, but instead an object. This mismatch causes `System.Text.Json` to throw the exception.

---

### **Diagnosis**
- The JSON you provided looks like a **single object**, not an array. If you are attempting to deserialize into a `List<CustomerContactEntity>`, the JSON should be an array like:
  ```json
  [
    {
      "accountNumber": "30125",
      "accountName": "3PLUS LOGISTICS",
      ...
    }
  ]
  ```

- If the JSON is a single object:
  ```json
  {
    "accountNumber": "30125",
    "accountName": "3PLUS LOGISTICS",
    ...
  }
  ```

You need to deserialize it into a **single instance** of `CustomerContactEntity`, not a list.

---

### **Fix Options**

#### **1. Deserialize Single Object**
If the JSON represents a single object, deserialize it directly:
```csharp
var customerContact = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<CustomerContactEntity>(jsonResponse, new JsonSerializerOptions
{
    PropertyNameCaseInsensitive = true
});
```

---

#### **2. Deserialize a List**
If the JSON is an array, ensure the format matches:
```json
[
  {
    "accountNumber": "30125",
    "accountName": "3PLUS LOGISTICS",
    ...
  }
]
```

Deserialize as follows:
```csharp
var customerContacts = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<List<CustomerContactEntity>>(jsonResponse, new JsonSerializerOptions
{
    PropertyNameCaseInsensitive = true
});
```

---

#### **3. Identify JSON Structure**
Use this code to inspect the JSON and ensure the structure matches expectations:
```csharp
Console.WriteLine(jsonResponse);
```

---

### **Update to Class**
If the JSON is a **nested structure** or includes unrelated fields, ensure your class matches the structure.

For example, if `customerBillingJson` is part of another entity:
```json
{
  "customerBillingJson": {
    "accountNumber": "30125",
    "accountName": "3PLUS LOGISTICS",
    ...
  }
}
```

You may need an additional wrapper class:
```csharp
public class WrapperEntity
{
    [JsonPropertyName("customerBillingJson")]
    public CustomerContactEntity CustomerBillingJson { get; set; }
}
```

Deserialize with:
```csharp
var wrapper = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<WrapperEntity>(jsonResponse, new JsonSerializerOptions
{
    PropertyNameCaseInsensitive = true
});
var customerContact = wrapper.CustomerBillingJson;
```

---

### **Key Takeaways**
- Check if the JSON is a single object or an array.
- Match the deserialization type (`CustomerContactEntity` vs. `List<CustomerContactEntity>`).
- Adjust the JSON or your class structure as necessary.

To exclude empty fields dynamically when creating `mainAddress` and `mainContact`, you can check each field before including it in the output. Here's the updated code:

### **Updated Code with Empty Fields Excluded**
```csharp
var mainAddress = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]
{
    contacts.AccountAddress1,
    contacts.AccountAddress2,
    contacts.AccountAddress3,
    $"{contacts.AccountCity}, {contacts.AccountState} {contacts.AccountZip}"
}.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));

var mainContact = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]
{
    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactName) 
        ? $"{contacts.AccountContactName} ({contacts.AccountContactTitle})" : null,
    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactPhone) 
        ? $"Phone: {contacts.AccountContactPhone}" : null,
    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountPhone2) 
        ? $"Alternate Phone: {contacts.AccountPhone2}" : null,

        ? $"Email: {contacts.AccountContactEmail}" : null,
    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactFax) 
        ? $"Fax: {contacts.AccountContactFax}" : null,
    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactText) 
        ? $"Notes: {contacts.AccountContactText}" : null
}.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));
```

1. **Use of `string.Join`**:
   - Joins all non-empty fields with a line feed (`Environment.NewLine`).

2. **Filtering Empty Fields**:
   - The `Where` clause ensures only non-empty and non-whitespace values are included.

3. **Dynamic Handling**:
   - Each field is conditionally included based on its value.

## USER
thisis our winform for now.  i have a new textbox. i want to filter the grid in realtime as data si typed in the textox. it should filter either name or account #

## ASSISTANT
To implement a real-time filter for the grid based on input from a `TextBox`, you can leverage the `TextChanged` event of the `TextBox` and apply a filter to the grid's data source. Here's how you can achieve this:

---

### **Steps to Implement**

1. **Add a `TextBox` to the Form**:
   - Place a `TextBox` above or near the grid in the form designer.
   - Name it, e.g., `txtFilter`.

2. **Handle the `TextChanged` Event**:
   - Attach an event handler to the `TextChanged` event of the `TextBox`.

3. **Filter the Grid**:
   - Use LINQ to filter the data source in real-time.
   - Apply the filtered results back to the grid.

---

### **Updated Code**

#### **Form Designer Changes**
```csharp
// Add this in the form designer or initialize manually
private System.Windows.Forms.TextBox txtFilter;

private void InitializeComponent()
{
    // Other initializations...

    txtFilter = new System.Windows.Forms.TextBox();
    txtFilter.Location = new System.Drawing.Point(20, 20); // Adjust as needed
    txtFilter.Width = 200;
    txtFilter.TextChanged += TxtFilter_TextChanged;

    this.Controls.Add(txtFilter);

    // Other controls...
}
```

#### **Filtering Logic**
```csharp
private List<CustomerContactEntity> _originalData; // Store the original unfiltered data

private async void CustomerDashboard_Load(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    try
    {
        // Load the original data
        _originalData = (await _customerRepository.GetAllCustomersAsync()).ToList();
        radGridView1.DataSource = _originalData;
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        MessageBox.Show($"Error loading customers: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
    }
}

private void TxtFilter_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    var filterText = txtFilter.Text.ToLower();

    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(filterText))
    {
        // Reset to original data if filter is cleared
        radGridView1.DataSource = _originalData;
        return;
    }

    // Filter the data based on account number or name
    var filteredData = _originalData
        .Where(c => (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(c.AccountNumber) && c.AccountNumber.ToLower().Contains(filterText)) ||
                    (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(c.AccountName) && c.AccountName.ToLower().Contains(filterText)))
        .ToList();

    radGridView1.DataSource = filteredData;
}
```

---

### **Explanation**

1. **TextBox Event**:
   - `TextChanged` is triggered every time the user types in the `TextBox`.

2. **Data Reset**:
   - When the filter text is cleared, the grid resets to the original dataset.

3. **Filtering Logic**:
   - Filters the data where either `AccountNumber` or `AccountName` contains the entered text.
   - Case-insensitive comparison using `ToLower()`.

4. **Real-Time Updates**:
   - The filtered results are displayed in the grid instantly as the user types.

---

### **Example Output**
**Initial Data:**
```
Account Number | Account Name
----------------------------------
30125          | 3PLUS LOGISTICS
30126          | TOUCHDOWN FREIGHT
```

**Filter Input:**
- **User types "301"**:
  ```
  Account Number | Account Name
  ----------------------------------
  30125          | 3PLUS LOGISTICS
  30126          | TOUCHDOWN FREIGHT
  ```

- **User types "logi"**:
  ```
  Account Number | Account Name
  ----------------------------------
  30125          | 3PLUS LOGISTICS
  ```

---

This approach ensures responsive filtering with minimal performance overhead. Let me know if you need additional modifications!

    public partial class CustomerDashboard : RadForm

    {

        private readonly ICustomerRepository _customerRepository;

        private List<CustomerEntity> _customerData; // Class-level dataset



        public CustomerDashboard(ICustomerRepository customerRepository)

        {

            _customerRepository = customerRepository ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customerRepository));

            InitializeComponent();



            // Disable automatic row sizing

            radGridView1.AutoSizeRows = false;



            // Attach events

            radGridView1.RowFormatting += RadGridView1_RowFormatting;

            radGridView1.CellEndEdit += RadGridView1_CellEndEdit;

            radGridView1.SelectionChanged += RadGridView1_SelectionChanged;



            // Load data when the form initializes

            Load += CustomerDashboard_Load;

        }



        // Load customer data into the grid on form load

        private async void CustomerDashboard_Load(object sender, EventArgs e)

        {

            try

            {

                _customerData = (await _customerRepository.GetAllCustomersAsync()).ToList();

                radGridView1.DataSource = _customerData;

            }

            catch (Exception ex)

            {

                MessageBox.Show($"Error loading customers: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);

            }

        }



        // Handle updates when a cell edit ends

        private async void RadGridView1_CellEndEdit(object sender, GridViewCellEventArgs e)

        {

            try

            {

                var editedCustomer = (CustomerEntity)e.Row.DataBoundItem;

                var success = await _customerRepository.UpdateCustomerAsync(editedCustomer);



                if (!success)

                {

                    MessageBox.Show("Failed to update customer.", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);

                }

            }

            catch (Exception ex)

            {

                MessageBox.Show($"Error updating customer: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);

            }

        }



        // Event handler for formatting rows dynamically

        private void RadGridView1_RowFormatting(object sender, RowFormattingEventArgs e)

        {

            // Implement custom row formatting logic if needed

        }



        // Handle row selection to populate customer details

        private void RadGridView1_SelectionChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)

        {

            var selectedRow = radGridView1.CurrentRow;

            if (selectedRow == null) return;



            var accountNumber = selectedRow.Cells["AccountNumber"].Value?.ToString();

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) return;



            // Find the corresponding record in the dataset

            var selectedCustomer = _customerData.FirstOrDefault(c => c.AccountNumber == accountNumber);

            if (selectedCustomer == null) return;



            valCustomerAccountName.Text = selectedCustomer.AccountName;

            valCustomerAcctountNumber.Text = selectedCustomer.AccountNumber;



            // Unpack the customer_address_json field

            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(selectedCustomer.BillingAddressJson))

            {

                try

                {

                    var contacts = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<CustomerContactEntity>(selectedCustomer.BillingAddressJson);

                    if (contacts != null)

                    {

                        // Example: Concatenate address and contact details

                        var mainAddress = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]

                              {

                            contacts.AccountAddress1,

                            contacts.AccountAddress2,

                            contacts.AccountAddress3,

                            contacts.AccountCity, 

                            contacts.AccountState,

                            contacts.AccountZip

                        }.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));



                        var mainContact = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]

                        {

                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactName)

                                ? $"{contacts.AccountContactName} ({contacts.AccountContactTitle})" : null,

                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactPhone)

                                ? $"Phone: {contacts.AccountContactPhone}" : null,

                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountPhone2)

                                ? $"Alternate Phone: {contacts.AccountPhone2}" : null,

                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactEmail)

                                ? $"Email: {contacts.AccountContactEmail}" : null,

                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactFax)

                                ? $"Fax: {contacts.AccountContactFax}" : null,

                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactText)

                                ? $"Notes: {contacts.AccountContactText}" : null

                        }.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));



                        // Populate the form fields

                        valCustomerMainAddress.Text = mainAddress;

                        valCustomerMainContact.Text = mainContact;

                    }

                }

                catch (Exception ex)

                {

                    MessageBox.Show($"Error unpacking customer JSON: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);

                }

            }

        }

    }

}

## ASSISTANT
If the `TxtFilter_TextChanged` event fires but does not update the grid, the issue might be related to how the grid's data source or filtering is being handled. Let's troubleshoot and fix this:

---

### **Debugging and Fixes**

#### **1. Ensure Data Source Supports Filtering**
Telerik's `RadGridView` filtering relies on its underlying data source. If `_customerData` is not compatible with filtering, the changes won't reflect.

- **Fix**: Bind the grid to a `BindingList` or `BindingSource`, which supports filtering:
```csharp
BindingSource bindingSource = new BindingSource();
bindingSource.DataSource = _customerData;
radGridView1.DataSource = bindingSource;
```

#### **2. Use Built-in Filtering Correctly**
Ensure the `FilterDescriptors` are correctly applied and the grid is refreshed:
```csharp
private void TxtFilter_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    var filterText = txtFilter.Text.ToLower();

    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(filterText))
    {
        radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear(); // Clear filters
        return;
    }

    // Apply filtering
    radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear();
    CompositeFilterDescriptor compositeFilter = new CompositeFilterDescriptor
    {
        LogicalOperator = FilterLogicalOperator.Or
    };

    compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountNumber", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));
    compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountName", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));

    radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Add(compositeFilter);

    // Force the grid to refresh
    radGridView1.MasterTemplate.Refresh();
}
```

#### **3. Verify Column Names**
Ensure the column names `AccountNumber` and `AccountName` match exactly with the `RadGridView` columns. If they don't:
- Update the `FilterDescriptor` keys to match the column names.

#### **4. Check Data Loading**
If the grid's data source isn't updated after filtering:
- Ensure `_customerData` has data.
- Confirm the `txtFilter.TextChanged` event is properly attached:
```csharp
txtFilter.TextChanged += TxtFilter_TextChanged;
```

---

### **Updated Filtering Code**
```csharp
private void TxtFilter_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    try
    {
        var filterText = txtFilter.Text.ToLower();

        if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(filterText))
        {
            radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear();
            return;
        }

        radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear();
        CompositeFilterDescriptor compositeFilter = new CompositeFilterDescriptor
        {
            LogicalOperator = FilterLogicalOperator.Or
        };

        compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountNumber", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));
        compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountName", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));

        radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Add(compositeFilter);
        radGridView1.MasterTemplate.Refresh(); // Refresh grid
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        MessageBox.Show($"Error applying filter: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
    }
}
```

---

### **Steps to Validate**
1. Verify `_customerData` contains records and is set as the `DataSource`.
2. Confirm the column names (`AccountNumber`, `AccountName`) are correctly spelled in the grid and match the property names.
3. Check the `TxtFilter_TextChanged` event handler is properly attached.
4. Test with a minimal filter text and observe if the grid updates.

really using System;
using System.Collections.Generic;
using System.Linq;
using System.Text.Json;
using System.Windows.Forms;
using Logistics.App.Models.customers;
using Logistics.App.Persistance.Interfaces;
using Telerik.WinControls.UI;

namespace Logistics.GUI.Forms.Customer
{
    public partial class CustomerDashboard : RadForm
    {
        private readonly ICustomerRepository _customerRepository;
        private List<CustomerEntity> _customerData; // Class-level dataset

        public CustomerDashboard(ICustomerRepository customerRepository)
        {
            _customerRepository = customerRepository ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(customerRepository));
            InitializeComponent();

            // Disable automatic row sizing
            radGridView1.AutoSizeRows = false;

            // Attach events
            radGridView1.RowFormatting += RadGridView1_RowFormatting;
            radGridView1.CellEndEdit += RadGridView1_CellEndEdit;
            radGridView1.SelectionChanged += RadGridView1_SelectionChanged;

            // Attach the TextChanged event for filtering
            txtFilter.TextChanged += TxtFilter_TextChanged;

            // Load data when the form initializes
            Load += CustomerDashboard_Load;
        }

        // Load customer data into the grid on form load
        private async void CustomerDashboard_Load(object sender, EventArgs e)
        {
            try
            {
                _customerData = (await _customerRepository.GetAllCustomersAsync()).ToList();
                radGridView1.DataSource = _customerData;
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                MessageBox.Show($"Error loading customers: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
            }
        }

        // Handle updates when a cell edit ends
        private async void RadGridView1_CellEndEdit(object sender, GridViewCellEventArgs e)
        {
            try
            {
                var editedCustomer = (CustomerEntity)e.Row.DataBoundItem;
                var success = await _customerRepository.UpdateCustomerAsync(editedCustomer);

                if (!success)
                {
                    MessageBox.Show("Failed to update customer.", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
                }
            }
            catch (Exception ex)
            {
                MessageBox.Show($"Error updating customer: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
            }
        }

        // Event handler for formatting rows dynamically
        private void RadGridView1_RowFormatting(object sender, RowFormattingEventArgs e)
        {
            // Implement custom row formatting logic if needed
        }

        // Handle row selection to populate customer details
        private void RadGridView1_SelectionChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
        {
            var selectedRow = radGridView1.CurrentRow;
            if (selectedRow == null) return;

            var accountNumber = selectedRow.Cells["AccountNumber"].Value?.ToString();
            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) return;

            // Find the corresponding record in the dataset
            var selectedCustomer = _customerData.FirstOrDefault(c => c.AccountNumber == accountNumber);
            if (selectedCustomer == null) return;

            valCustomerAccountName.Text = selectedCustomer.AccountName;
            valCustomerAcctountNumber.Text = selectedCustomer.AccountNumber;

            // Unpack the customer_address_json field
            if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(selectedCustomer.BillingAddressJson))
            {
                try
                {
                    var contacts = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<CustomerContactEntity>(selectedCustomer.BillingAddressJson);
                    if (contacts != null)
                    {
                        // Example: Concatenate address and contact details
                        var mainAddress = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]
                              {
                            contacts.AccountAddress1,
                            contacts.AccountAddress2,
                            contacts.AccountAddress3,
                            contacts.AccountCity, 
                            contacts.AccountState,
                            contacts.AccountZip
                        }.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));

                        var mainContact = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]
                        {
                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactName)
                                ? $"{contacts.AccountContactName} ({contacts.AccountContactTitle})" : null,
                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactPhone)
                                ? $"Phone: {contacts.AccountContactPhone}" : null,
                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountPhone2)
                                ? $"Alternate Phone: {contacts.AccountPhone2}" : null,
                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactEmail)
                                ? $"Email: {contacts.AccountContactEmail}" : null,
                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactFax)
                                ? $"Fax: {contacts.AccountContactFax}" : null,
                            !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactText)
                                ? $"Notes: {contacts.AccountContactText}" : null
                        }.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));

                        // Populate the form fields
                        valCustomerMainAddress.Text = mainAddress;
                        valCustomerMainContact.Text = mainContact;
                    }
                }
                catch (Exception ex)
                {
                    MessageBox.Show($"Error unpacking customer JSON: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
                }
            }
        }

        private void TxtFilter_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
        {
            var filterText = txtFilter.Text.ToLower();

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(filterText))
            {
                // Reset to the original data if filter is cleared
                radGridView1.DataSource = _customerData;
                return;
            }

            // Use RadGridView built-in filtering
            radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear();
            var compositeFilter = new CompositeFilterDescriptor();

            compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountNumber", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));
            compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountName", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));

            compositeFilter.LogicalOperator = FilterLogicalOperator.Or;
            radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Add(compositeFilter);
        }
    }
}

To resolve the issue of the grid not updating after filtering, you might need to ensure the data binding is appropriately managed. Here's what you can do:

### **Fix Approach**

1. **Rebind the Data Source**:
   Even though `FilterDescriptors` works on the `MasterTemplate`, sometimes reassigning the `DataSource` can resolve the issue:
   ```csharp
   radGridView1.DataSource = null;
   radGridView1.DataSource = _customerData;
   ```

2. **Explicit Data Refresh**:
   If filtering doesn't immediately reflect, explicitly call `radGridView1.Refresh()`:
   ```csharp
   radGridView1.Refresh();
   ```

3. **Verify Column Names**:
   Ensure that `AccountNumber` and `AccountName` match exactly with the column names in the `RadGridView`. Column names are case-sensitive.

4. **Debug the FilterText**:
   Ensure the `filterText` is correctly set and valid:
   ```csharp
   Debug.WriteLine($"Filter Text: {filterText}");
   ```

---

### **Updated Code**
```csharp
private void TxtFilter_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    var filterText = txtFilter.Text.ToLower();

    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(filterText))
    {
        // Reset to the original data if filter is cleared
        radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear(); // Clear filters
        radGridView1.MasterTemplate.Refresh(); // Refresh the grid
        return;
    }

    // Apply filtering
    radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear();
    CompositeFilterDescriptor compositeFilter = new CompositeFilterDescriptor
    {
        LogicalOperator = FilterLogicalOperator.Or
    };

    compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountNumber", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));
    compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountName", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));

    radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Add(compositeFilter);

    // Rebind and refresh the grid
    radGridView1.DataSource = null;
    radGridView1.DataSource = _customerData;
    radGridView1.Refresh();
}
```

---

### **Validation Checklist**
- Ensure `_customerData` is populated and not null or empty.
- Verify column names in the `RadGridView`.
- Call `Refresh()` after applying `FilterDescriptors`.

This approach ensures filtering works dynamically as expected. Let me know if you encounter further issues!

## ASSISTANT
It appears you're encountering an issue where the `RadGridView` in your `CustomerDashboard` form doesn't refresh as expected after applying filters, even though you've implemented the `TxtFilter_TextChanged` event handler to manage filtering based on user input.

**Potential Causes and Solutions:**

1. **Data Binding and Collection Types:**
   - **Issue:** If `_customerData` is a standard `List<CustomerEntity>`, changes to the collection may not automatically notify the UI to refresh.
   - **Solution:** Consider using an `ObservableCollection<CustomerEntity>` instead of a `List<CustomerEntity>`. The `ObservableCollection` class implements the `INotifyCollectionChanged` interface, which notifies the UI of any additions, deletions, or modifications to the collection.

     ```csharp
     private ObservableCollection<CustomerEntity> _customerData;
     ```

     Ensure that when you retrieve data asynchronously, you initialize `_customerData` appropriately:

     ```csharp
     private async void CustomerDashboard_Load(object sender, EventArgs e)
     {
         try
         {
             var customers = await _customerRepository.GetAllCustomersAsync();
             _customerData = new ObservableCollection<CustomerEntity>(customers);
             radGridView1.DataSource = _customerData;
         }
         catch (Exception ex)
         {
             MessageBox.Show($"Error loading customers: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
         }
     }
     ```

2. **Filter Application and UI Refresh:**
   - **Issue:** After applying filters, the grid may not automatically refresh to reflect the filtered data.
   - **Solution:** After setting the `FilterDescriptors`, explicitly call the `MasterTemplate.Refresh()` method to force the grid to redraw and display the filtered data.

     ```csharp
     private void TxtFilter_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
     {
         var filterText = txtFilter.Text.ToLower();

         radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Clear();

         if (!string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(filterText))
         {
             var compositeFilter = new CompositeFilterDescriptor
             {
                 LogicalOperator = FilterLogicalOperator.Or
             };

             compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountNumber", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));
             compositeFilter.FilterDescriptors.Add(new FilterDescriptor("AccountName", FilterOperator.Contains, filterText));

             radGridView1.MasterTemplate.FilterDescriptors.Add(compositeFilter);
         }

         // Force the grid to refresh
         radGridView1.MasterTemplate.Refresh();
     }
     ```

3. **Data Source Reset:**
   - **Issue:** Setting the `DataSource` to `null` and then reassigning it can disrupt data binding and may not be necessary.
   - **Solution:** Instead of resetting the `DataSource`, ensure that the collection itself notifies the UI of changes. Using `ObservableCollection` as mentioned above helps in achieving this.

**Additional Considerations:**

- **Asynchronous Data Loading:** Ensure that the asynchronous data loading in `CustomerDashboard_Load` completes before any filtering operations are performed. This prevents scenarios where filtering is applied to an uninitialized or empty data set.

- **Thread Safety:** If data loading or filtering operations involve background threads, ensure that any updates to the UI components are invoked on the main UI thread to prevent cross-thread operation exceptions.

By implementing these changes, your `RadGridView` should properly refresh and display the filtered data as expected.

here is the correct way using System;

using System.Drawing;

using Telerik.QuickStart.WinControls;

using Telerik.WinControls.UI;



namespace Telerik.Examples.WinControls.GridView.Filtering.CustomFiltering

{

    public partial class Form1 : ExamplesForm

    {

        

        public Form1()

        {

            InitializeComponent();



            this.radGridView.EnableGrouping = false;

            this.radGridView.EnableHotTracking = true;

            this.radGridView.ShowFilteringRow = false;

            this.radGridView.EnableFiltering = true;

            this.radGridView.EnableCustomFiltering = true;            

        }



        protected override void OnLoad(EventArgs e)

        {

            base.OnLoad(e);



            this.customersTableAdapter.Fill(this.nwindRadGridView.Customers);



            this.SelectedControl = this.filterTextBox;

        }



        private void radGridView1_CustomFiltering(object sender, GridViewCustomFilteringEventArgs e)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(this.filterTextBox.Text))

            {

                this.radGridView.BeginUpdate();

                e.Visible = true;

                for (int i = 0; i < this.radGridView.ColumnCount; i++)

                {

                    e.Row.Cells[i].Style.Reset();                   

                }



                this.radGridView.EndUpdate(false);

                return;

            }

            this.radGridView.BeginUpdate();

            e.Visible = false;

            for (int i = 0; i < this.radGridView.ColumnCount; i++)

            {

                string text = e.Row.Cells[i].Value.ToString();

                if (text.IndexOf(this.filterTextBox.Text, 0, StringComparison.InvariantCultureIgnoreCase) >= 0)

                {

                    e.Visible = true;

                    e.Row.Cells[i].Style.CustomizeFill = true;

                    e.Row.Cells[i].Style.DrawFill = true;

                    e.Row.Cells[i].Style.BackColor = Color.FromArgb(201, 252, 254);

                }

                else

                {

                    e.Row.Cells[i].Style.Reset();                    

                }



                this.radGridView.EndUpdate(false);

            }

        }



        private void filterTextBox_TextChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)

        {

            this.radGridView.MasterTemplate.Refresh();

        }



        protected override void WireEvents()

        {

            this.radGridView.CustomFiltering += new Telerik.WinControls.UI.GridViewCustomFilteringEventHandler(this.radGridView1_CustomFiltering);

            this.filterTextBox.TextChanged += new System.EventHandler(this.filterTextBox_TextChanged);

        }

    }

}

### Design Principles:

1. **Harmonic Core Structure**:

3. **Avoiding Meltdown**:
   - To prevent runaway conditions, the feedback loop limits the energy input into the system to ensure the harmonic ratio of 0.35 is never exceeded. Excessive electromagnetic dominance triggers corrections to reassert strong nuclear forces.

This correction amplifies strong nuclear interactions over electromagnetic ones, realigning the harmonic ratio and preventing a meltdown.

{\n            if (string.IsNullOrEmpty(txtFilter.Text))\n            {\n                e.Visible = true;\n                return;\n            }\n\n            e.Visible = false;\n            foreach (var cell in e.Row.Cells)\n            {\n                if (cell.Value != null && cell.Value.ToString().IndexOf(txtFilter.Text, StringComparison.InvariantCultureIgnoreCase) >= 0)\n                {\n                    e.Visible = true;\n                    cell.Style.CustomizeFill = true;\n                    cell.Style.DrawFill = true;\n                    cell.Style.BackColor = System.Drawing.Color.FromArgb(201, 252, 254);\n                }\n                else\n                {\n                    cell.Style.Reset();\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}"}]}

## USER
add a method to get a list based on the customer account number using System;

using System.Collections.Generic;

using System.Net.Http;

using System.Text;

using System.Text.Json;

using System.Threading.Tasks;

using Logistics.App.Models.customers;

using Logistics.App.Persistance.Interfaces;



namespace Logistics.App.Persistance.Customers

{

    public class ConsigneeRepository : IConsigneeRepository

    {

        private readonly HttpClient _httpClient;



        public ConsigneeRepository(HttpClient httpClient)

        {

            _httpClient = httpClient ?? throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(httpClient));

        }



        // Add a new consignee

        public async Task<bool> AddConsigneeAsync(ConsigneeEntity consignee)

        {

            if (consignee == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(consignee));



            var jsonContent = new StringContent(JsonSerializer.Serialize(consignee), Encoding.UTF8, "application/json");

            var response = await _httpClient.PostAsync("/consignee/add", jsonContent);



            return response.IsSuccessStatusCode;

        }



        // Retrieve a consignee by ID

        public async Task<ConsigneeEntity> GetConsigneeByIdAsync(string id)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(id)) throw new ArgumentException("ID cannot be null or empty.", nameof(id));



            var response = await _httpClient.GetAsync($"/consignee/{id}");



            if (!response.IsSuccessStatusCode) throw new Exception("Error fetching consignee by ID.");



            var jsonResponse = await response.Content.ReadAsStringAsync();

            return JsonSerializer.Deserialize<ConsigneeEntity>(jsonResponse, new JsonSerializerOptions { PropertyNameCaseInsensitive = true });

        }



        // Retrieve all consignees

        public async Task<IEnumerable<ConsigneeEntity>> GetConsigneesAsync()

        {

            var response = await _httpClient.GetAsync("/consignee/all");



            if (!response.IsSuccessStatusCode) throw new Exception("Error fetching consignees.");



            var jsonResponse = await response.Content.ReadAsStringAsync();

            return JsonSerializer.Deserialize<IEnumerable<ConsigneeEntity>>(jsonResponse, new JsonSerializerOptions { PropertyNameCaseInsensitive = true });

        }



        // Update an existing consignee

        public async Task<bool> UpdateConsigneeAsync(ConsigneeEntity consignee)

        {

            if (consignee == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(consignee));



            var jsonContent = new StringContent(JsonSerializer.Serialize(consignee), Encoding.UTF8, "application/json");

            var response = await _httpClient.PutAsync("/consignee/update", jsonContent);



            return response.IsSuccessStatusCode;

        }



        // Mark a consignee as deleted

        public async Task<bool> DeleteConsigneeAsync(string id)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(id)) throw new ArgumentException("ID cannot be null or empty.", nameof(id));



            var response = await _httpClient.DeleteAsync($"/consignee/delete/{id}");



            return response.IsSuccessStatusCode;

        }

    }

}

// Routes for Consignee

// Add a new consignee

app.MapPost("/consignee/add", async (ConsigneeEntity consignee, IConsigneeRepository repository) =>

{

    if (consignee == null) return Results.BadRequest("Consignee cannot be null.");

    try

    {

        var result = await repository.AddConsigneeAsync(consignee);

        return result ? Results.Ok("Consignee added successfully.") : Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

    catch (Exception ex)

    {

        Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while adding a consignee.");

        return Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

});

// Retrieve a consignee by ID

app.MapGet("/consignee/{id}", async (string id, IConsigneeRepository repository) =>

{

    try

    {

        var consignee = await repository.GetConsigneeByIdAsync(id);

        return consignee != null ? Results.Ok(consignee) : Results.NotFound("Consignee not found.");

    }

    catch (Exception ex)

    {

        Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while retrieving consignee.");

        return Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

});

// Retrieve all consignees

app.MapGet("/consignee/all", async (IConsigneeRepository repository) =>

{

    try

    {

        var consignees = await repository.GetConsigneesAsync();

        return Results.Ok(consignees);

    }

    catch (Exception ex)

    {

        Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while retrieving consignees.");

        return Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

});

// Update an existing consignee

app.MapPut("/consignee/update", async (ConsigneeEntity consignee, IConsigneeRepository repository) =>

{

    if (consignee == null) return Results.BadRequest("Consignee cannot be null.");

    try

    {

        var result = await repository.UpdateConsigneeAsync(consignee);

        return result ? Results.Ok("Consignee updated successfully.") : Results.NotFound("Consignee not found.");

    }

    catch (InvalidOperationException ex)

    {

        Log.Warning(ex, "Concurrency conflict occurred.");

        return Results.Conflict(ex.Message);

    }

    catch (Exception ex)

    {

        Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while updating consignee.");

        return Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

});

// Mark a consignee as deleted

app.MapDelete("/consignee/delete/{id}", async (string id, IConsigneeRepository repository) =>

{

    try

    {

        var result = await repository.DeleteConsigneeAsync(id);

        return result ? Results.Ok("Consignee marked as deleted.") : Results.NotFound("Consignee not found.");

    }

    catch (Exception ex)

    {

        Log.Error(ex, "Error occurred while marking consignee as deleted.");

        return Results.StatusCode(500);

    }

});

## USER
nice. add the function you createdhere to the repository using Logistix.ServerAPI.Data.Context;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Customers;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Models.Entity;

using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;

using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;

using System;

using System.Collections.Generic;

using System.Linq;

using System.Threading.Tasks;



namespace Logistix.ServerAPI.Data.Repository

{

    public class ConsigneeRepository : IConsigneeRepository

    {

        private readonly IDbContextFactory<CustomerContext> _dbContextFactory;

        private readonly ILogger<ConsigneeRepository> _logger;



        public ConsigneeRepository(IDbContextFactory<CustomerContext> dbContextFactory, ILogger<ConsigneeRepository> logger)

        {

            _dbContextFactory = dbContextFactory;

            _logger = logger;

        }



        // Add a new consignee 

        public async Task<bool> AddConsigneeAsync(ConsigneeEntity consignee)

        {

            if (consignee == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(consignee));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            context.Set<ConsigneeEntity>().Add(consignee);

            await context.SaveChangesAsync();

            return true;

        }



        // Retrieve a consignee  by ID

        public async Task<ConsigneeEntity> GetConsigneeByIdAsync(string Number)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(Number)) throw new ArgumentException(" number cannot be null or empty.", nameof(Number));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            return await context.Set<ConsigneeEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.AccountNumber == Number);

        }



        // Retrieve all consignee s

        public async Task<IEnumerable<ConsigneeEntity>> GetConsigneesAsync()

        {

            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            return await context.Set<ConsigneeEntity>().ToListAsync();

        }



        // Update an existing consignee 

        public async Task<bool> UpdateConsigneeAsync(ConsigneeEntity consignee)

        {

            if (consignee == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(consignee));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            var existing = await context.Set<ConsigneeEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.AccountNumber == consignee.AccountNumber);

            if (existing == null) return false;



            context.Entry(existing).CurrentValues.SetValues(consignee);



            try

            {

                await context.SaveChangesAsync();

                return true;

            }

            catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)

            {

                throw new InvalidOperationException("The consignee  was updated by another user.");

            }

        }



        // Mark a consignee  as deleted by setting Deleted to true

        public async Task<bool> DeleteConsigneeAsync(string Number)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(Number)) throw new ArgumentException(" number cannot be null or empty.", nameof(Number));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            var consignee = await context.Set<ConsigneeEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.AccountNumber == Number);

            if (consignee == null) return false;



            consignee.AccountDeleted = true;



            try

            {

                await context.SaveChangesAsync();

                return true;

            }

            catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)

            {

                throw new InvalidOperationException("The consignee  was deleted or updated by another user.");

            }

        }

    }

}

same here using Logistix.ServerAPI.Data.Context;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Interfaces.Customers;

using Logistix.ServerAPI.Models.Entity;

using Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore;

using Microsoft.Extensions.Logging;

using System;

using System.Collections.Generic;

using System.Linq;

using System.Threading.Tasks;



namespace Logistix.ServerAPI.Data.Repository

{

    public class ShipperRepository : IShipperRepository

    {

        private readonly IDbContextFactory<CustomerContext> _dbContextFactory;

        private readonly ILogger<ShipperRepository> _logger;



        public ShipperRepository(IDbContextFactory<CustomerContext> dbContextFactory, ILogger<ShipperRepository> logger)

        {

            _dbContextFactory = dbContextFactory;

            _logger = logger;

        }



        // Add a new shipper account

        public async Task<bool> AddShipperAsync(ShipperEntity shipperAccount)

        {

            if (shipperAccount == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(shipperAccount));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            context.Set<ShipperEntity>().Add(shipperAccount);

            await context.SaveChangesAsync();

            return true;

        }



        // Retrieve a shipper account by Account Number

        public async Task<ShipperEntity> GetShipperByIdAsync(string accountNumber)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) throw new ArgumentException("Account number cannot be null or empty.", nameof(accountNumber));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            return await context.Set<ShipperEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.AccountNumber == accountNumber);

        }



        // Retrieve all shipper accounts

        public async Task<IEnumerable<ShipperEntity>> GetShippersAsync()

        {

            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            return await context.Set<ShipperEntity>().ToListAsync();

        }



        // Update an existing shipper account

        public async Task<bool> UpdateShipperAsync(ShipperEntity shipperAccount)

        {

            if (shipperAccount == null) throw new ArgumentNullException(nameof(shipperAccount));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            var existingAccount = await context.Set<ShipperEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.AccountNumber == shipperAccount.AccountNumber);

            if (existingAccount == null) return false;



            context.Entry(existingAccount).CurrentValues.SetValues(shipperAccount);



            try

            {

                await context.SaveChangesAsync();

                return true;

            }

            catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)

            {

                throw new InvalidOperationException("The shipper account was updated by another user.");

            }

        }



        // Mark a shipper account as deleted by setting AccountStats to 'Deleted'

        public async Task<bool> DeleteShipperAsync(string accountNumber)

        {

            if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) throw new ArgumentException("Account number cannot be null or empty.", nameof(accountNumber));



            using var context = _dbContextFactory.CreateDbContext();

            var shipperAccount = await context.Set<ShipperEntity>().FirstOrDefaultAsync(c => c.AccountNumber == accountNumber);

            if (shipperAccount == null) return false;



            shipperAccount.AccountStats = "Deleted";



            try

            {

                await context.SaveChangesAsync();

                return true;

            }

            catch (DbUpdateConcurrencyException)

            {

                throw new InvalidOperationException("The shipper account was deleted or updated by another user.");

            }

        }

    }

}

## ASSISTANT
Here's the updated implementation for handling the selection change in the main grid, which updates both `consigneeGrid` and `shipperGrid` based on the selected main grid row:

```csharp
private async void RadGridView1_SelectionChanged(object sender, EventArgs e)
{
    var selectedRow = radGridView1.CurrentRow;
    if (selectedRow == null) return;

    var accountNumber = selectedRow.Cells["AccountNumber"].Value?.ToString();
    if (string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(accountNumber)) return;

    // Find the corresponding customer from the dataset
    var selectedCustomer = _customerData.FirstOrDefault(c => c.AccountNumber == accountNumber);
    if (selectedCustomer == null) return;

    // Update consigneeGrid
    try
    {
        var consignees = await _consigneeRepository.GetConsigneesByAccountNumberAsync(accountNumber);
        consigneeGrid.DataSource = consignees.ToList();
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        MessageBox.Show($"Error loading consignees: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
    }

    // Update shipperGrid
    try
    {
        var shippers = await _shipperRepository.GetShippersByAccountNumberAsync(accountNumber);
        shipperGrid.DataSource = shippers.ToList();
    }
    catch (Exception ex)
    {
        MessageBox.Show($"Error loading shippers: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
    }

    // Populate other form fields
    valCustomerAccountName.Text = selectedCustomer.AccountName;
    valCustomerAcctountNumber.Text = selectedCustomer.AccountNumber;

    // Unpack and display billing address JSON
    if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(selectedCustomer.BillingAddressJson))
    {
        try
        {
            var contacts = JsonSerializer.Deserialize<CustomerContactEntity>(selectedCustomer.BillingAddressJson);
            if (contacts != null)
            {
                var mainAddress = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]
                {
                    contacts.AccountAddress1,
                    contacts.AccountAddress2,
                    contacts.AccountAddress3,
                    contacts.AccountCity,
                    contacts.AccountState,
                    contacts.AccountZip
                }.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));

                var mainContact = string.Join(Environment.NewLine, new[]
                {
                    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactName) ? $"{contacts.AccountContactName} ({contacts.AccountContactTitle})" : null,
                    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactPhone) ? $"Phone: {contacts.AccountContactPhone}" : null,
                    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountPhone2) ? $"Alternate Phone: {contacts.AccountPhone2}" : null,
                    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactEmail) ? $"Email: {contacts.AccountContactEmail}" : null,
                    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactFax) ? $"Fax: {contacts.AccountContactFax}" : null,
                    !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(contacts.AccountContactText) ? $"Notes: {contacts.AccountContactText}" : null
                }.Where(field => !string.IsNullOrWhiteSpace(field)));

                valCustomerMainAddress.Text = mainAddress;
                valCustomerMainContact.Text = mainContact;
            }
        }
        catch (Exception ex)
        {
            MessageBox.Show($"Error unpacking customer JSON: {ex.Message}", "Error", MessageBoxButtons.OK, MessageBoxIcon.Error);
        }
    }
}
```

### Explanation:
1. **Consignee Grid Update**:
   - Asynchronously fetches the consignees related to the `accountNumber`.
   - Sets the data source of `consigneeGrid` to the retrieved list.

2. **Shipper Grid Update**:
   - Similar to consignees, asynchronously fetches shippers and updates the `shipperGrid`.

3. **Error Handling**:
   - Displays appropriate messages if there are issues fetching data for consignees or shippers.

4. **Billing Address Parsing**:
   - The JSON parsing and display logic remain the same, updated with proper error handling.

This approach ensures that both grids are updated dynamically, and the error handling provides clear feedback in case of issues. Let me know if you need further modifications or enhancements!
